<a id='question-083'></a>

### Question 083: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte oxidative burst and increased susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 是一种原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合物的缺陷，导致吞噬细胞的氧化爆发受损，并增加对 catalase 阳性生物的易感性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 5-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections since infancy. His infections include pneumonia (Staphylococcus aureus, Aspergillus fumigatus), skin abscesses (Serratia marcescens), osteomyelitis (Burkholderia cepacia), and lymphadenitis. Family history reveals that his maternal grandfather also had recurrent infections requiring multiple hospitalizations. Physical examination shows multiple draining skin abscesses and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory studies reveal normal serum immunoglobulin levels (IgG, IgA, IgM) and normal lymphocyte counts. A dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay on neutrophils shows absent fluorescence upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA), indicating a defective respiratory burst.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective synthesis of the Fc receptor gamma chain (CD32), leading to impaired opsonization and phagocytosis.
(B) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) shared by several cytokine receptors, leading to impaired T-cell and NK-cell development.
(C) Defective synthesis of a subunit of the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired production of reactive oxygen species (ROS) by phagocytes.
(D) Defective synthesis of the CD40 ligand (CD40L) on T cells, leading to impaired B-cell class switching and T-cell-dependent antigen presentation.
(E) Defective synthesis of the C5 convertase, leading to impaired formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC) and increased susceptibility to Neisseria infections.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe bacterial and fungal infections since infancy:** Suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function. The types of organisms (S. aureus, Aspergillus, Serratia, Burkholderia) are catalase-positive, which is a key clue.
- **Skin abscesses, pneumonia, osteomyelitis, lymphadenitis:** Common sites for infections caused by phagocyte defects.
- **Family history of recurrent infections:** Suggests a genetic basis, potentially X-linked or autosomal recessive.
- **Normal serum immunoglobulin levels and lymphocyte counts:** Rules out humoral immunodeficiencies (like CVID, XLA) and severe combined immunodeficiencies (SCID) affecting lymphocyte development.
- **Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay shows absent fluorescence upon PMA stimulation:** This is the diagnostic test for Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). PMA stimulates the respiratory burst in normal neutrophils, causing DHR oxidation and fluorescence. Absence of fluorescence indicates a defective respiratory burst.
- **Catalase-positive organisms:** These organisms produce catalase, an enzyme that breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2). Phagocytes normally use H2O2 and the enzyme myeloperoxidase to generate hypochlorous acid (bleach), a potent antimicrobial agent. In CGD, the lack of ROS production makes phagocytes unable to kill catalase-positive organisms effectively.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** Caused by mutations in genes encoding subunits of the NADPH oxidase complex (gp91phox/NOX2, p22phox/CYBB, p47phox/NCF1, p67phox/NCF2, p40phox/NCF4). This complex is located in the phagosomal membrane of neutrophils and macrophages.
- **NADPH Oxidase Function:** Its primary role is to generate superoxide radicals (O2-) by transferring electrons from NADPH to molecular oxygen (O2). Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, including hydrogen peroxide (H2O2), hydroxyl radicals (•OH), and hypochlorous acid (HOCl).
- **Respiratory Burst:** The rapid production of ROS by phagocytes is called the respiratory burst or oxidative burst. It is crucial for killing ingested microbes, especially catalase-positive bacteria and fungi.
- **DHR Assay:** Dihydrorhodamine is a non-fluorescent dye. When oxidized by ROS produced during the respiratory burst, it becomes fluorescent rhodamine. The DHR assay measures the ability of neutrophils to produce ROS.
- **Pathophysiology:** In CGD, the defective NADPH oxidase prevents the generation of ROS. Phagocytes can ingest microbes but cannot effectively kill them, leading to the formation of granulomas (collections of activated macrophages and lymphocytes attempting to contain the infection) and recurrent infections.
- **Inheritance:** Most common form is X-linked recessive (mutation in CYBB gene encoding gp91phox), accounting for ~70% of cases. Autosomal recessive forms exist (mutations in NCF1, NCF2, NCF4, CYBA).
- **Infections:** Characterized by infections with catalase-positive organisms like Staphylococcus aureus, Serratia marcescens, Burkholderia cepacia, Aspergillus species, Nocardia species, and Rhodococcus species.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective Fc receptor gamma chain (CD32):** CD32 is the low-affinity Fc receptor for IgG. Defects in CD32 are rare and typically associated with impaired phagocytosis of IgG-opsonized particles, but not the specific pattern of catalase-positive infections or the absent DHR fluorescence seen in CGD. This defect would primarily affect opsonization-dependent phagocytosis.
- (B) **Defective common gamma chain (γc):** The γc (encoded by IL2RG) is shared by receptors for IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. Defects lead to X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID), characterized by absent T cells and NK cells, and severely impaired B-cell function. This patient has normal lymphocyte counts and immunoglobulin levels, ruling out SCID.
- (D) **Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L):** CD40L (encoded by CD40LG) on activated T cells binds to CD40 on B cells, antigen-presenting cells (APCs), and other cells. Defects lead to X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal or elevated IgM levels but low IgG, IgA, and IgE levels due to impaired B-cell class switching and defective T-cell-dependent immune responses. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections, particularly Pneumocystis jirovecii. This patient has normal immunoglobulin levels.
- (E) **Defective C5 convertase:** The C5 convertase (C5b-6-7-8-9) cleaves C5 into C5a and C5b. C5b initiates the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC). Defects in the terminal complement pathway (C5-C9) lead to increased susceptibility to Neisseria infections (e.g., Neisseria meningitidis, Neisseria gonorrhoeae) because the MAC is crucial for lysing these bacteria. While CGD patients can have Neisseria infections, it's not the defining feature, and the DHR assay would be normal in complement deficiency. The key organisms in this vignette are catalase-positive bacteria and fungi, characteristic of CGD.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked SCID (γc deficiency) | Hyper-IgM Syndrome (CD40L deficiency) | Complement Deficiency (Terminal Pathway) | Fc Receptor Deficiency (e.g., CD32) |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH Oxidase Complex                | Common Gamma Chain (γc)      | CD40 Ligand (CD40L)                  | C5-C9 Components                        | Fc Receptor (e.g., CD32)           |
| **Cell Type Affected**  | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells, NK cells            | T cells, B cells                     | All cells expressing complement receptors | Phagocytes                         |
| **Key Mechanism**       | Impaired Respiratory Burst (ROS)     | Impaired Lymphocyte Development | Impaired Class Switching, T-dependent immunity | Impaired MAC Formation                 | Impaired Opsonization/Phagocytosis |
| **Diagnostic Test**     | DHR Flow Cytometry (Absent Fluorescence) | Flow Cytometry (Absent T/NK) | Serum Ig Levels (High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE) | CH50 Assay (Low)                        | Specific Fc Receptor Assays        |
| **Characteristic Infections** | Catalase-positive bacteria/fungi (S. aureus, Aspergillus, Serratia, Burkholderia) | Opportunistic (Viral, Fungal, Bacterial) | Opportunistic (Pneumocystis, Cryptosporidium) | Neisseria (meningitidis, gonorrhoeae) | Variable, often encapsulated bacteria |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR           | X-linked                    | X-linked                             | AR, somatic mutations                    | AR, AD                             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocyte function due to a defective NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired ROS production and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The DHR flow cytometry assay is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from other primary immunodeficiencies affecting phagocytes (e.g., Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency), T cells (e.g., SCID, DiGeorge), B cells (e.g., XLA, CVID, Hyper-IgM), and complement. Remember the characteristic infections and diagnostic tests for each.

<a id='question-084'></a>

### Question 084: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 34 (catalase)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD), a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte function and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (本题考察慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD)，一种原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合体缺陷，导致吞噬细胞功能受损，并易感于 catalase 阳性微生物。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 10-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections, including pneumonia, skin abscesses, and lymphadenitis. His mother reports that he has had multiple hospitalizations for similar infections since infancy, often requiring prolonged antibiotic therapy. Family history is significant for a maternal uncle who died at age 15 from complications of overwhelming infection. Physical examination reveals multiple draining skin lesions, hepatosplenomegaly, and cervical lymphadenopathy. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A skin biopsy from an abscess reveals numerous neutrophils surrounding necrotic tissue, but these neutrophils lack the ability to effectively kill ingested bacteria. Flow cytometry analysis of neutrophils shows normal expression of CD11b/CD18 (complement receptor 3) and CD16 (FcγRIII). A dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay, which measures the production of reactive oxygen species (ROS) by neutrophils upon stimulation, shows markedly reduced fluorescence compared to normal controls. Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective synthesis of superoxide dismutase, leading to accumulation of superoxide radicals and oxidative damage.
(B) Impaired phagosome-lysosome fusion, preventing the delivery of antimicrobial enzymes to ingested pathogens.
(C) Deficiency in the NADPH oxidase complex, resulting in decreased production of superoxide anions.
(D) Defective expression of the CD40 ligand (CD40L) on T cells, impairing T cell-dependent B cell activation and antibody production.
(E) Impaired synthesis of catalase, leading to an inability to break down hydrogen peroxide and increased oxidative stress.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe bacterial and fungal infections since infancy:** Suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function.
- **Skin abscesses, pneumonia, lymphadenitis:** Common manifestations of impaired phagocytic killing, especially by catalase-positive organisms.
- **Family history of early death from infection:** Points towards a genetic disorder, potentially X-linked (given maternal uncle affected).
- **Neutrophilia:** Common response to infection, but neutrophils are functionally impaired.
- **Skin biopsy: Neutrophils surrounding necrotic tissue but unable to kill bacteria:** Classic finding in CGD; neutrophils can phagocytose but cannot generate ROS to kill ingested microbes.
- **Normal CD11b/CD18 and CD16 expression:** Rules out Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD) and FcγRIII deficiency, respectively.
- **Markedly reduced DHR fluorescence:** Directly indicates impaired ROS production by neutrophils, the hallmark of CGD.
- **Catalase-positive organisms:** The question implicitly tests the link between CGD and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms, as these organisms produce catalase, which breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) into water and oxygen. Phagocytes normally use H2O2 (converted to hypochlorous acid by myeloperoxidase) as a potent antimicrobial agent. In CGD, H2O2 accumulates in the phagosome but cannot be converted to hypochlorous acid efficiently due to the lack of superoxide production, and catalase-producing organisms can effectively neutralize the H2O2, further hindering killing.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** A primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages).
- **Pathophysiology:** Caused by mutations in genes encoding subunits of the NADPH oxidase complex (e.g., *CYBB* for X-linked CGD, *CYBA*, *NCF1*, *NCF2*, *NCF4* for autosomal recessive CGD).
- **NADPH Oxidase Function:** This enzyme complex is located in the phagosomal membrane and catalyzes the reduction of molecular oxygen (O2) to superoxide anion (O2•−) using NADPH as an electron donor.
    O2 + NADPH → O2•− + NADP+ + H+
- **Reactive Oxygen Species (ROS) Production:** Superoxide anion is then converted to other ROS, including hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) by superoxide dismutase (SOD), and hypochlorous acid (HOCl) by myeloperoxidase (MPO) in the presence of chloride ions. These ROS are crucial for killing ingested pathogens.
- **Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) Assay:** DHR is a non-fluorescent dye that is oxidized to fluorescent rhodamine by ROS produced during neutrophil activation. Reduced fluorescence in CGD indicates defective ROS production.
- **Susceptibility to Catalase-Positive Organisms:** Organisms like *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, *Aspergillus* species, and *Nocardia* species produce catalase. Catalase breaks down H2O2 into water and oxygen. In normal phagocytes, H2O2 is converted to HOCl by MPO, a potent killing agent. In CGD, H2O2 accumulates but cannot be efficiently converted to HOCl, and catalase-producing organisms can further degrade the H2O2, making phagocytic killing ineffective.
- **Clinical Features:** Recurrent infections with catalase-positive bacteria and fungi, formation of granulomas (due to impaired killing leading to chronic inflammation) in various organs (lungs, skin, lymph nodes, liver, GI tract).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Defective synthesis of superoxide dismutase (SOD):** SOD converts superoxide (O2•−) to hydrogen peroxide (H2O2). Deficiency would lead to accumulation of superoxide, which *is* toxic, but it wouldn't explain the inability to produce ROS or the susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The DHR assay would likely be normal or even elevated if superoxide accumulated. This is incorrect.
- (B): **Impaired phagosome-lysosome fusion:** This defect, seen in Chediak-Higashi syndrome, prevents the delivery of lysosomal enzymes (like lysozyme, proteases) to the phagosome. While it impairs killing, the primary defect isn't ROS production, and the DHR assay would be normal. This is incorrect.
- (D): **Defective expression of CD40 ligand (CD40L):** CD40L on T cells interacts with CD40 on B cells, antigen-presenting cells, and other cells. Deficiency (X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome) impairs T cell-dependent B cell activation, class switching, and memory B cell formation, leading to susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria and opportunistic infections, but not primarily catalase-positive organisms or granuloma formation due to phagocyte dysfunction. Phagocyte function (ROS production) is normal. This is incorrect.
- (E): **Impaired synthesis of catalase:** Catalase is produced by host cells (like neutrophils) and by certain pathogens. Host catalase deficiency is not a known primary immunodeficiency. If the *pathogen* lacked catalase, it would be *more* susceptible to killing by ROS, not less. The patient's problem is the *phagocyte's* inability to produce ROS, not a lack of host catalase. This is incorrect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD) | Chediak-Higashi Syndrome | X-linked Hyper-IgM Syndrome |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------|--------------------------------------|-----------------------------|------------------------------|
| **Primary Defect**   | NADPH oxidase complex               | CD11b/CD18 (β2 integrin)             | LYST gene (trafficking)     | CD40L                         |
| **Cell Affected**    | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | Phagocytes (Neutrophils)             | Phagocytes, Melanocytes, etc.| T cells                       |
| **Key Function Impaired** | ROS production (killing)           | Adhesion, migration                  | Phagosome-lysosome fusion, granule exocytosis | T cell-dependent B cell help |
| **Characteristic Finding** | Impaired DHR oxidation             | Delayed umbilical cord separation, poor wound healing | Giant granules in leukocytes, oculocutaneous albinism | Elevated IgM, low IgG/IgA/IgE |
| **Typical Infections** | Catalase-positive bacteria/fungi    | Encapsulated bacteria               | Encapsulated bacteria       | Encapsulated bacteria, PCP    |
| **Granuloma Formation** | Yes                                  | No                                   | No                          | No                           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is characterized by defective NADPH oxidase leading to impaired ROS production and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The DHR assay is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from other phagocyte defects like LAD (adhesion/migration defect, normal ROS) and Chediak-Higashi (fusion defect, giant granules). Remember the link between CGD and catalase-positive pathogens.

<a id='question-085'></a>

### Question 085: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 35 (positive)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular defect in Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD), specifically the inability of phagocytes to produce reactive oxygen species (ROS) due to a defect in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired killing of catalase-positive organisms. (本题考察慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 的分子缺陷，即 NADPH 氧化酶复合体缺陷导致吞噬细胞无法产生活性氧 (ROS)，从而影响对 catalase 阳性微生物的杀伤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fever, cough, and shortness of breath. He has a history of recurrent pneumonia, skin abscesses, and lymphadenitis since infancy, often requiring hospitalization. His mother reports that infections typically involve organisms like *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, and *Aspergillus* species. Family history is significant for a similar presentation in his maternal grandfather, who died at age 25 from overwhelming sepsis. Physical examination reveals a cachectic boy with multiple draining skin lesions and cervical lymphadenopathy. Chest X-ray shows bilateral pulmonary infiltrates and cavitation. Sputum culture grows *Aspergillus fumigatus*. A peripheral blood smear shows neutrophils, but further testing reveals that these neutrophils fail to exhibit a respiratory burst upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA).

Which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for this patient's recurrent infections?

(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells, impairing B cell isotype switching and macrophage activation.
(B) Defective tyrosine kinase (Src family) signaling, leading to impaired T cell receptor (TCR) signaling and development.
(C) Defective NADPH oxidase complex assembly, preventing the generation of superoxide radicals.
(D) Defective integrin expression (CD18), impairing leukocyte adhesion and migration to sites of infection.
(E) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA) activity, leading to the accumulation of toxic metabolites in lymphocytes.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 6-year-old boy with recurrent, severe infections (pneumonia, abscesses, lymphadenitis) since infancy. This points towards a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function.
- **Infecting Organisms:** *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, and *Aspergillus fumigatus*. These are catalase-positive organisms. Catalase breaks down hydrogen peroxide, which is normally produced by phagocytes. Phagocytes also produce other ROS via the NADPH oxidase pathway. Failure to produce ROS effectively impairs killing of catalase-positive organisms, which can detoxify the H2O2 produced by the myeloperoxidase system. This pattern is highly suggestive of Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD).
- **Family History:** Similar presentation in maternal grandfather suggests an X-linked inheritance pattern, which is common for CGD (due to mutations in the *CYBB* gene encoding gp91phox).
- **Physical Exam:** Cachexia, draining skin lesions, lymphadenopathy, pulmonary infiltrates/cavitation are consistent with chronic granulomatous inflammation and severe infections characteristic of CGD.
- **Diagnostic Test:** Neutrophils fail to exhibit a respiratory burst upon PMA stimulation. PMA activates protein kinase C, which phosphorylates and activates NADPH oxidase. The absence of a respiratory burst confirms a defect in the NADPH oxidase pathway.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying molecular defect.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** A primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex. This complex is responsible for generating superoxide radicals (O2-) from molecular oxygen (O2) within phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages). Superoxide is then converted to other ROS like hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) and hypochlorous acid (HOCl) by enzymes like superoxide dismutase and myeloperoxidase, which are crucial for killing ingested microbes, especially catalase-positive bacteria and fungi.
- **NADPH Oxidase Complex:** Composed of several subunits, including gp91phox (encoded by *CYBB* gene on the X chromosome) and p22phox, p47phox, p67phox, and p40phox. Mutations in any of these genes can cause CGD. The most common form is X-linked (gp91phox defect).
- **Respiratory Burst:** The rapid release of ROS by phagocytes upon activation. Measured using tests like the dihydrorhodamine (DHR) assay or nitroblue tetrazolium (NBT) reduction test. Failure to show a respiratory burst (as seen in this patient) is diagnostic for CGD.
- **Pathogenesis:** Impaired ROS production leads to ineffective killing of catalase-positive pathogens. This results in recurrent infections (pneumonia, abscesses, osteomyelitis, lymphadenitis) and the formation of granulomas (collections of activated macrophages and lymphocytes) in various tissues as the immune system attempts to contain the infection.
- **Catalase-Positive Organisms:** *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, *Aspergillus* species, *Nocardia* species, *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. These organisms produce catalase, which breaks down H2O2, rendering the phagocyte's alternative killing mechanism (myeloperoxidase system) less effective. The NADPH oxidase-derived ROS are essential for killing these organisms.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells:** This describes X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. CD40L on activated T cells binds to CD40 on B cells, macrophages, and dendritic cells. This interaction is crucial for B cell isotype switching (to IgG, IgA, IgE), affinity maturation, and germinal center formation. It also activates macrophages. Patients have low IgG, IgA, IgE, normal or high IgM, and recurrent bacterial infections (especially encapsulated bacteria) and opportunistic infections (like *Pneumocystis jirovecii*). This does not fit the patient's specific pathogens (catalase-positive) or the lack of respiratory burst.
- (B) **Defective tyrosine kinase (Src family) signaling:** This describes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA) or Bruton's agammaglobulinemia, caused by mutations in the *BTK* gene. BTK is essential for B cell development. Patients lack mature B cells and immunoglobulins, leading to recurrent bacterial infections, particularly with encapsulated organisms. This does not explain the phagocyte defect or the specific pathogens seen in the vignette. While Src family kinases are involved in signaling pathways, a general defect here doesn't specifically cause the NADPH oxidase failure seen in CGD.
- (D) **Defective integrin expression (CD18):** This describes Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 1 (LAD-1). CD18 is the common beta-2 subunit of several integrins (LFA-1, Mac-1, p150,95) expressed on leukocytes. These integrins are crucial for leukocyte adhesion to endothelial cells and migration to sites of infection. Patients have recurrent bacterial infections (often without pus formation), delayed umbilical cord separation, and impaired wound healing. This does not explain the failure of the respiratory burst or the specific catalase-positive pathogens.
- (E) **Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA) activity:** This describes Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID) due to ADA deficiency. ADA is an enzyme involved in purine metabolism. Deficiency leads to the accumulation of toxic metabolites (deoxyadenosine triphosphate) primarily in lymphocytes, causing severe lymphopenia and profound defects in both T and B cell immunity. Patients present early in life with severe, recurrent infections (bacterial, viral, fungal, opportunistic). This is a combined immunodeficiency affecting lymphocytes, not primarily phagocyte function, and doesn't explain the specific pattern of infections or the respiratory burst defect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked Hyper-IgM Syndrome | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) | Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD-1) | ADA-SCID                     |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- | :--------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH Oxidase Complex               | CD40L                       | BTK                               | CD18 (β2 integrin subunit)             | Adenosine Deaminase (ADA)   |
| **Affected Cells**      | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells, B cells, Macrophages | B cells                           | Leukocytes (Neutrophils, etc.)        | Lymphocytes (T & B cells)    |
| **Key Pathogens**       | Catalase-positive (Staph, Serratia, Burkholderia, Aspergillus) | Encapsulated bacteria, PCP        | Encapsulated bacteria                  | Bacteria (often no pus)         | Broad range (bacterial, viral, fungal) |
| **Hallmark Finding**    | Impaired Respiratory Burst          | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE   | Absent B cells, Low Ig             | Impaired Leukocyte Adhesion/Migration | Severe Lymphopenia           |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR          | X-linked                   | X-linked                          | Autosomal Recessive                    | Autosomal Recessive          |
| **Granuloma Formation** | Yes                                 | No                          | No                                | No                                     | No                           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is characterized by a defect in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired ROS production and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The key diagnostic finding is the failure of phagocytes to exhibit a respiratory burst. Differentiate CGD from other primary immunodeficiencies based on the affected cell type, specific pathogens, and immunological hallmarks (e.g., immunoglobulin levels, lymphocyte counts, adhesion defects). Remember the classic catalase-positive pathogens associated with CGD.

<a id='question-086'></a>

### Question 086: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 36 (bacteria)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte oxidative burst and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 是一种原发性免疫缺陷，其特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合物的缺陷，导致吞噬细胞的氧化爆发受损，并易感于 catalase 阳性生物。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 10-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of recurrent, non-bloody diarrhea, abdominal pain, and weight loss. He has a history of multiple severe bacterial and fungal infections since infancy, including pneumonia (Staphylococcus aureus, Aspergillus fumigatus), osteomyelitis (Serratia marcescens), and skin abscesses (Burkholderia cepacia). Family history is significant for a similarly affected maternal uncle who died in his 20s from complications of chronic infection. Physical examination reveals a cachectic male child with hepatosplenomegaly and multiple draining lymph nodes in the neck and axillae. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A neutrophil function test using dihydrorhodamine (DHR) 123 oxidation shows minimal fluorescence upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA), indicating a severely impaired oxidative burst.

Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective synthesis of the Fc receptor gamma chain (CD32), leading to impaired antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC).
(B) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc), leading to impaired signaling through cytokine receptors.
(C) Defective synthesis of a subunit of the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired production of reactive oxygen species (ROS).
(D) Defective synthesis of the CD40 ligand (CD40L), leading to impaired T-cell dependent B-cell activation and macrophage activation.
(E) Defective synthesis of the C5 convertase, leading to impaired formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 10-year-old boy with recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections since infancy (pneumonia, osteomyelitis, abscesses). This points towards a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocyte function or T-cell immunity.
*   **Infecting Organisms:** *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus fumigatus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*. These are catalase-positive organisms (except *Aspergillus*, which produces catalase-like enzymes). Catalase breaks down hydrogen peroxide, which is a key product of the phagocyte oxidative burst. Patients with impaired oxidative burst are particularly susceptible to catalase-positive organisms because the bacteria can neutralize the ROS produced by the phagocytes.
*   **Family History:** Affected maternal uncle suggests an X-linked inheritance pattern, common in some primary immunodeficiencies (like CGD and X-linked SCID).
*   **Physical Exam:** Cachexia, hepatosplenomegaly, draining lymph nodes indicate chronic inflammation and infection.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Leukocytosis with neutrophilia suggests an ongoing bacterial infection.
*   **Neutrophil Function Test (DHR 123):** Minimal fluorescence upon PMA stimulation indicates a severely impaired oxidative burst. DHR 123 is oxidized to fluorescent rhodamine by ROS produced during the respiratory burst. Lack of fluorescence confirms the defect.
*   **Synthesis:** The constellation of recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms, family history suggestive of X-linked inheritance, and impaired neutrophil oxidative burst strongly points to Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). CGD is caused by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
*   **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** This is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages). The underlying defect lies in the NADPH oxidase enzyme complex, located in the phagosomal membrane. This complex is responsible for generating superoxide radicals (O2-) from molecular oxygen (O2) during the respiratory burst. Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, including hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) and hypochlorous acid (HOCl), which are crucial for killing ingested microbes, especially catalase-positive bacteria and fungi.
*   **NADPH Oxidase Complex:** This complex consists of several subunits: gp91phox (NOX2, encoded by the *CYBB* gene on the X chromosome - X-linked CGD), p22phox (encoded by *CYBA*), p47phox (encoded by *NCF1*), p67phox (encoded by *NCF2*), and p40phox (encoded by *NCF4*). Defects in any of these subunits can cause CGD. The most common form is X-linked (gp91phox defect).
*   **Mechanism of Susceptibility:** Without functional NADPH oxidase, phagocytes cannot produce ROS. Catalase-positive organisms (like *S. aureus*, *Serratia*, *Burkholderia*, *Aspergillus*) produce catalase, which breaks down H2O2. This allows these organisms to survive within phagocytes, leading to persistent infection and granuloma formation (hence the name "Chronic Granulomatous Disease").
*   **DHR Test:** Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) 123 is a non-fluorescent dye that is oxidized by ROS to fluorescent rhodamine 123. In normal neutrophils, PMA stimulation activates NADPH oxidase, leading to ROS production and oxidation of DHR 123, resulting in increased fluorescence. In CGD neutrophils, the NADPH oxidase is defective, ROS production is absent or minimal, and DHR 123 remains largely unoxidized, resulting in minimal fluorescence.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective synthesis of the Fc receptor gamma chain (CD32), leading to impaired antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC).** CD32 is the FcγRII receptor, primarily found on B cells, monocytes, and macrophages. It binds IgG. Defects in CD32 are associated with impaired B-cell signaling and potentially reduced ADCC, but not the specific pattern of infections (especially catalase-positive organisms) or the impaired oxidative burst seen in this patient. This describes a defect in humoral immunity/innate immunity signaling, not phagocyte killing mechanism.
- (B) **Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc), leading to impaired signaling through cytokine receptors.** The common gamma chain (γc, encoded by *IL2RG* gene on the X chromosome) is a component of receptors for several cytokines, including IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. Defects in γc cause X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID), characterized by severe T-cell and NK-cell deficiency, leading to susceptibility to a wide range of opportunistic infections (viral, fungal, bacterial, protozoal) early in life. This patient's presentation (specific organisms, impaired oxidative burst) is inconsistent with X-SCID.
- (D) **Defective synthesis of the CD40 ligand (CD40L), leading to impaired T-cell dependent B-cell activation and macrophage activation.** CD40L (CD154, encoded by *CD40LG* gene on the X chromosome) is expressed on activated T cells and interacts with CD40 on B cells, dendritic cells, and macrophages. Defects in CD40L cause X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal or elevated IgM levels but deficient IgG, IgA, and IgE production due to impaired class switching and somatic hypermutation. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections, particularly *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. This does not fit the clinical picture of impaired phagocyte killing and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms.
- (E) **Defective synthesis of the C5 convertase, leading to impaired formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC).** The C5 convertase is an enzyme complex in the complement cascade that cleaves C5 into C5a and C5b. C5b initiates the formation of the MAC (C5b-9), which lyses pathogens. Defects in the terminal complement pathway (C5-C9) lead to increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria, particularly *Neisseria* species. This does not explain the impaired oxidative burst or the specific pattern of infections seen in this patient.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked SCID (γc deficiency) | X-linked Hyper-IgM (CD40L deficiency) | Complement Deficiency (C5-C9) |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :---------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :----------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH Oxidase Complex                | Common Gamma Chain (γc)       | CD40 Ligand (CD40L)                   | Terminal Complement (C5-C9)   |
| **Affected Cells**      | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells, NK cells             | B cells, Macrophages                 | All cells (systemic)          |
| **Key Mechanism**       | Impaired Oxidative Burst (ROS)       | Impaired T-cell Development  | Impaired B-cell Class Switching      | Impaired MAC Formation         |
| **Characteristic Infections** | Catalase-positive bacteria/fungi   | Broad range (opportunistic)   | *Pneumocystis jirovecii*, bacteria   | Encapsulated bacteria (*Neisseria*) |
| **Diagnostic Test**     | DHR/Nitroblue Tetrazolium (NBT) test | Flow cytometry (T/NK cells)   | IgM levels, IgG/IgA/IgE levels        | CH50 assay                    |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR           | X-linked                     | X-linked                             | AR, somatic mutations         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a phagocyte disorder characterized by impaired oxidative burst due to defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The DHR test is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from other primary immunodeficiencies affecting phagocytes (e.g., Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency) or adaptive immunity (e.g., SCID, Hyper-IgM syndrome, CVID, complement deficiencies) based on the specific pattern of infections and underlying immunological defect.

<a id='question-087'></a>

### Question 087: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 37 (Recurrent)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular defect in Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD), specifically the impaired function of NADPH oxidase, leading to defective phagosome maturation and inability to generate reactive oxygen species (ROS) necessary for killing certain intracellular pathogens. (本题考察慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 的分子缺陷，即 NADPH 氧化酶功能障碍，导致吞噬体成熟受损，无法产生杀死某些胞内病原体所需的活性氧 (ROS)。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fever, cough, and shortness of breath. His mother reports that he has had recurrent pneumonia, skin abscesses, and lymphadenitis since infancy, often requiring hospitalization. He was born full-term without complications. Family history is significant for a maternal uncle who died at age 10 from overwhelming sepsis. Physical examination reveals a cachectic boy with multiple draining skin lesions on his trunk and extremities, cervical lymphadenopathy, and bibasilar crackles on lung auscultation. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A chest X-ray confirms bilateral pneumonia with multiple cavitary lesions. Sputum culture grows *Staphylococcus aureus* and *Aspergillus fumigatus*. A neutrophil function test, specifically the dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay, shows absent fluorescence upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA).

What is the underlying molecular defect responsible for this patient's recurrent infections?

(A) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) shared by multiple cytokine receptors.
(B) Impaired DNA double-strand break repair leading to increased chromosomal instability and susceptibility to specific viral infections.
(C) Defective expression of CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T cells, preventing isotype switching and germinal center formation.
(D) Defective assembly and activation of the NADPH oxidase enzyme complex in phagocytes.
(E) Defective expression of the CD154 molecule on T cells, leading to impaired B cell activation and antibody production.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent infections since infancy:** Suggests a primary immunodeficiency. The types of infections (pneumonia, skin abscesses, lymphadenitis) point towards defects in phagocytic function or T-cell immunity.
- **Specific pathogens (*Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus fumigatus*):** These are catalase-positive organisms. Phagocytes normally kill catalase-positive organisms using the respiratory burst, generating reactive oxygen species (ROS). Recurrent infections with these organisms strongly suggest a defect in the respiratory burst.
- **Family history (maternal uncle died young from sepsis):** Suggests a possible X-linked or autosomal recessive inheritance pattern.
- **Neutrophil function test (DHR assay):** The DHR assay measures the production of ROS by neutrophils. Absent fluorescence upon PMA stimulation indicates a failure of the respiratory burst. PMA activates protein kinase C, which phosphorylates and activates NADPH oxidase.
- **Cavitary lung lesions:** Characteristic finding in CGD due to granuloma formation and necrosis in response to infection, particularly with *Aspergillus*.
- **Putting it together:** The clinical picture of recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms (especially *S. aureus* and *Aspergillus*), cavitary lung lesions, family history, and a positive DHR test (indicating defective respiratory burst) is classic for Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** A primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase enzyme complex.
- **NADPH Oxidase Function:** This multi-subunit enzyme complex is located in the phagosome membrane of phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages). Its function is to catalyze the transfer of electrons from NADPH to molecular oxygen (O2), generating superoxide anion (O2•−). Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, such as hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) and hypochlorous acid (HOCl), which are highly toxic to ingested microbes.
- **Molecular Defect in CGD:** Mutations in any of the five genes encoding the subunits of NADPH oxidase (gp91phox/NOX2, p22phox/CYBB, p47phox/NCF1, p67phox/NCF2, p40phox/NCF4) can cause CGD. The most common form is X-linked (gp91phox defect), explaining the family history pattern.
- **Consequence of Defect:** Phagocytes in CGD patients can ingest microbes but cannot effectively kill catalase-positive organisms because they cannot produce ROS. This leads to the formation of granulomas (attempted containment) and recurrent, severe infections.
- **DHR Assay:** Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) is a non-fluorescent dye. When oxidized by ROS produced during the respiratory burst, it becomes fluorescent rhodamine. In CGD, neutrophils fail to produce ROS, so DHR remains non-fluorescent. Flow cytometry measures this fluorescence after stimulation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) shared by multiple cytokine receptors:** This describes X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID). Patients with X-SCID have profound defects in T-cell and NK-cell development due to impaired signaling through IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21 receptors. They present early in life with severe opportunistic infections (viral, fungal, bacterial) and failure to thrive, not typically with recurrent catalase-positive infections and granulomas. The DHR assay would be normal in X-SCID.
- (B) **Impaired DNA double-strand break repair leading to increased chromosomal instability and susceptibility to specific viral infections:** This describes Nijmegen breakage syndrome, caused by mutations in the NBS1 gene. It is characterized by immunodeficiency (primarily affecting T cells), microcephaly, facial dysmorphism, and increased risk of lymphoma and leukemia. While there is immunodeficiency, the specific pattern of infections (catalase-positive) and the DHR test result are inconsistent with this diagnosis.
- (C) **Defective expression of CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T cells, preventing isotype switching and germinal center formation:** This describes X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal T-cell numbers but impaired T-cell help to B cells, leading to low levels of IgG, IgA, and IgE, but normal or elevated IgM. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections, particularly *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. The DHR assay would be normal, and the infection pattern differs from CGD.
- (E) **Defective expression of the CD154 molecule on T cells, leading to impaired B cell activation and antibody production:** CD154 is another name for CD40L. Therefore, this option describes the same condition as option (C), X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. The reasoning for its incorrectness is the same as for option (C).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked SCID (γc deficiency) | X-linked Hyper-IgM (CD40L deficiency) | Nijmegen Breakage Syndrome |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :-------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH oxidase function              | γc signaling                 | CD40L expression                     | DNA double-strand break repair |
| **Immune Cell Affected** | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells, NK cells            | T cells (help to B cells)            | T cells                     |
| **Key Pathogens**       | Catalase-positive (e.g., *S. aureus*, *Aspergillus*) | Opportunistic (Viral, Fungal, Bacterial) | Opportunistic (e.g., *Pneumocystis*) | Viral                      |
| **Characteristic Findings** | Granulomas, abscesses, cavitary lesions | Severe infections early in life | High IgM, low IgG/IgA/IgE            | Microcephaly, facial dysmorphism |
| **DHR Assay Result**    | Absent fluorescence                 | Normal fluorescence          | Normal fluorescence                  | Normal fluorescence         |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR          | X-linked                    | X-linked                             | Autosomal Recessive         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a phagocyte defect characterized by recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms due to impaired respiratory burst. The DHR assay is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from other primary immunodeficiencies based on the specific immune cell affected, the pattern of infections, and laboratory findings (e.g., DHR assay, immunoglobulin levels, lymphocyte subsets). Key differentials include SCID (severe T-cell deficiency), Hyper-IgM syndrome (T-cell help defect), and other phagocyte disorders.

<a id='question-088'></a>

### Question 088: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defective phagocyte NADPH oxidase activity, leading to impaired production of reactive oxygen species (ROS) necessary for killing certain ingested pathogens. (慢性肉芽肿病是一种原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是吞噬细胞NADPH氧化酶活性缺陷，导致无法产生杀死某些摄入病原体所需的活性氧 (ROS)。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 5-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections, including pneumonia, skin abscesses, and osteomyelitis, starting shortly after birth. His infections are often caused by catalase-positive organisms such as *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, and *Aspergillus* species. Family history reveals that his maternal grandfather also had recurrent infections requiring hospitalization. Physical examination shows multiple draining skin abscesses and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory studies reveal normal lymphocyte counts and immunoglobulin levels, but neutrophils fail to exhibit a respiratory burst upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA) in a dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay. A biopsy of a lung lesion shows granulomas with multinucleated giant cells, but no evidence of caseous necrosis. Which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for this patient's condition?

(A) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells, impairing B cell class switching and macrophage activation.
(B) Defective tyrosine kinase activity in T cells, leading to impaired T cell receptor (TCR) signaling and development.
(C) Defective NADPH oxidase subunits, preventing the generation of superoxide radicals within phagocytes.
(D) Defective synthesis of immunoglobulins due to mutations in B cell receptor signaling pathways.
(E) Defective complement component C3, leading to impaired opsonization and formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe bacterial and fungal infections starting early in life:** Suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function.
- **Specific pathogens (*S. aureus*, *Serratia*, *Burkholderia*, *Aspergillus*):** These are catalase-positive organisms. Catalase breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2), a key ROS produced by phagocytes. Patients with defects in ROS production are particularly susceptible to these organisms because they cannot effectively kill them within phagosomes.
- **Family history:** Suggests a genetic basis for the immunodeficiency, possibly X-linked or autosomal recessive.
- **Normal lymphocyte counts and immunoglobulin levels:** Rules out combined immunodeficiencies (like SCID) or primary antibody deficiencies (like CVID or XLA).
- **Neutrophils fail to exhibit a respiratory burst (DHR assay):** This is the hallmark diagnostic test for Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). The DHR assay measures the production of ROS (specifically superoxide) by neutrophils upon stimulation. Failure to produce ROS indicates a defect in the NADPH oxidase complex.
- **Granulomas with giant cells, no caseous necrosis:** Granulomas form as the immune system attempts to wall off pathogens that cannot be effectively killed by phagocytes. The lack of caseous necrosis distinguishes these granulomas from those seen in tuberculosis.
- **The question asks for the molecular defect:** Based on the clinical presentation and diagnostic findings, the defect lies in the phagocyte's ability to produce ROS.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** Caused by mutations in genes encoding subunits of the NADPH oxidase enzyme complex (e.g., *CYBB* for gp91phox, *CYBA* for p22phox, *NCF1*, *NCF2*, *NCF4*).
- **NADPH Oxidase Function:** This enzyme complex is located in the phagosomal membrane of neutrophils, macrophages, and other phagocytes. Its function is to catalyze the transfer of electrons from NADPH to molecular oxygen (O2), generating superoxide radicals (O2•−). Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, such as hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) by superoxide dismutase, and hypochlorous acid (HOCl) by myeloperoxidase.
- **Role of ROS:** ROS are crucial for the "oxidative burst" or "respiratory burst," which is essential for killing ingested bacteria and fungi within the phagolysosome.
- **Pathogenesis of CGD:** Defective NADPH oxidase prevents the generation of ROS. Phagocytes can ingest pathogens but cannot effectively kill catalase-positive organisms. This leads to chronic inflammation, granuloma formation (attempted containment), and recurrent infections.
- **Inheritance:** Most commonly X-linked recessive (gp91phox subunit defect), but autosomal recessive forms also exist.
- **Diagnosis:** Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay is the standard diagnostic test. PMA or other stimuli activate NADPH oxidase; in normal neutrophils, DHR is oxidized to fluorescent rhodamine. In CGD neutrophils, there is no fluorescence. Nitroblue tetrazolium (NBT) test is an older test based on the same principle.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) expression on T cells:** This causes X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have normal or elevated IgM levels but low IgG, IgA, and IgE levels due to impaired B cell class switching and defective T cell-dependent macrophage activation. They are susceptible to opportunistic infections, particularly *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. This does not fit the patient's presentation (normal immunoglobulins, specific pathogens, failed respiratory burst).
- (B) **Defective tyrosine kinase activity in T cells:** This describes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA) if Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) is affected, or other T cell signaling defects. XLA causes a lack of B cells and immunoglobulins, leading to recurrent bacterial infections (especially encapsulated organisms). T cell tyrosine kinase defects would primarily affect T cell function. Neither fits the patient's presentation (normal lymphocytes and immunoglobulins, phagocyte defect).
- (D) **Defective synthesis of immunoglobulins due to mutations in B cell receptor signaling pathways:** This describes various primary antibody deficiencies, such as Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID) or XLA (if BTK is involved). These patients have low immunoglobulin levels and are susceptible to encapsulated bacteria. This does not fit the patient's presentation (normal immunoglobulins, phagocyte defect).
- (E) **Defective complement component C3:** C3 deficiency is a severe immunodeficiency affecting both innate and adaptive immunity. It leads to impaired opsonization, chemotaxis, and MAC formation. Patients are highly susceptible to recurrent pyogenic bacterial infections, particularly with encapsulated organisms like *Neisseria meningitidis* and *Streptococcus pneumoniae*. This does not fit the patient's presentation (normal immunoglobulins, specific pathogens, failed respiratory burst).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked Hyper-IgM Syndrome | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) | CVID | C3 Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :--- | :------------ |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH Oxidase                        | CD40L                       | BTK                               | B cell defects | C3 protein    |
| **Immune Cell Affected** | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T cells, B cells            | B cells                          | B cells | All arms      |
| **Key Pathogens**       | Catalase+ bacteria, *Aspergillus*    | *Pneumocystis jirovecii*   | Encapsulated bacteria            | Encapsulated bacteria | Encapsulated bacteria |
| **Immunoglobulins**     | Normal                               | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE   | Very Low/Absent                  | Low   | Normal        |
| **Diagnostic Test**     | DHR/NBT assay (failed respiratory burst) | IgM levels, CD40L expression | B cell count, Ig levels, BTK expression | Ig levels, B cell count | CH50, C3 levels |
| **Granulomas**          | Yes                                  | No                         | No                               | No   | No            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocyte function due to a defect in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired ROS production. This results in susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms and granuloma formation. The DHR assay is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from antibody deficiencies (low Ig), T cell deficiencies (variable Ig, T cell defects), and complement deficiencies (impaired opsonization/MAC).

<a id='question-089'></a>

### Question 089: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 39 (infections,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is an inherited primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte oxidative burst and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 是一种遗传性原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合物的缺陷，导致吞噬细胞的氧化爆发受损，并易感于 catalase 阳性微生物。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 5-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections. His infections began shortly after birth, including persistent *Staphylococcus aureus* skin abscesses, pneumonia caused by *Serratia marcescens*, and invasive aspergillosis. He has a history of granuloma formation in multiple organs, including the lungs, liver, and lymph nodes, noted on previous imaging studies. Family history is significant for a similarly affected maternal uncle who died in childhood from overwhelming sepsis. Physical examination reveals multiple draining skin lesions, hepatosplenomegaly, and lymphadenopathy. Laboratory studies show markedly elevated erythrocyte sedimentation rate (ESR) and C-reactive protein (CRP). A neutrophil function test, specifically the dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay, demonstrates absent oxidative burst upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA).

Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective synthesis of the heavy chain of immunoglobulins, leading to impaired opsonization.
(B) Impaired T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the ZAP-70 kinase.
(C) Defective phagosome-lysosome fusion, resulting in inability to degrade ingested microbes.
(D) Defective production of reactive oxygen species (ROS) by phagocytes due to mutations in subunits of the NADPH oxidase complex.
(E) Defective complement component C3 convertase activity, leading to impaired bacterial lysis and opsonization.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 5-year-old boy with recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections starting early in life. This points towards a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocytes, as these infections are common in such conditions.
- **Infection Types:** *Staphylococcus aureus* (skin abscesses), *Serratia marcescens* (pneumonia), and *Aspergillus* (invasive aspergillosis). These organisms are catalase-positive. Catalase breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2), which is a key product of the phagocyte oxidative burst used to kill microbes. Patients with impaired oxidative burst are particularly susceptible to catalase-positive organisms because these organisms can detoxify the H2O2 produced by the phagocytes.
- **Granuloma Formation:** Granulomas in multiple organs (lungs, liver, lymph nodes) are a hallmark of Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). They form as the immune system attempts to wall off organisms that phagocytes cannot effectively kill.
- **Family History:** Affected maternal uncle suggests an X-linked inheritance pattern, which is common for CGD (specifically the most common form, due to mutations in the *CYBB* gene encoding gp91phox).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated ESR and CRP indicate significant inflammation due to ongoing infections.
- **Neutrophil Function Test (DHR Assay):** Absent oxidative burst upon PMA stimulation is the definitive diagnostic test for CGD. PMA activates protein kinase C, which phosphorylates and activates the NADPH oxidase complex. The DHR assay measures the production of ROS (specifically superoxide) by neutrophils. Absence of ROS production confirms a defect in the oxidative burst pathway.
- **Conclusion:** The constellation of early-onset recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms, granuloma formation, family history suggestive of X-linked inheritance, and absent neutrophil oxidative burst strongly points to Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** CGD is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocytes (neutrophils, monocytes, macrophages). The underlying defect lies in the NADPH oxidase complex, a multi-subunit enzyme complex located in the phagosomal membrane. This complex is responsible for generating superoxide radicals (O2•−) from molecular oxygen (O2) during the respiratory burst. Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, such as hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) by superoxide dismutase, and hypochlorous acid (HOCl) by myeloperoxidase. These ROS are crucial for killing ingested bacteria and fungi within the phagolysosome.
- **NADPH Oxidase Complex:** The complex consists of several subunits: gp91phox (encoded by *CYBB* gene on the X chromosome, most common defect), p22phox (encoded by *CYBA* gene, autosomal recessive), p47phox (encoded by *NCF1* gene, autosomal recessive), p67phox (encoded by *NCF2* gene, autosomal recessive), and p40phox (encoded by *NCF4* gene, autosomal recessive). Mutations in any of these genes can cause CGD.
- **Mechanism of Defect:** In CGD, the NADPH oxidase complex is either absent, non-functional, or unable to assemble properly on the phagosomal membrane. This prevents the generation of ROS, impairing the ability of phagocytes to kill certain microorganisms, particularly catalase-positive bacteria and fungi.
- **Catalase-Positive Organisms:** Organisms like *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, *Aspergillus*, and *Candida* produce the enzyme catalase. Catalase breaks down H2O2 into water and oxygen. In normal phagocytes, H2O2 is a potent microbicidal agent. However, in CGD phagocytes, H2O2 production is severely reduced. Catalase-positive organisms can further detoxify the small amounts of H2O2 that might be produced or present, effectively rendering the phagocyte's killing mechanism ineffective.
- **Granuloma Formation:** The inability to kill ingested microbes leads to persistent inflammation and the formation of granulomas, which are aggregates of macrophages, lymphocytes, and other inflammatory cells attempting to contain the infection.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Defective synthesis of the heavy chain of immunoglobulins, leading to impaired opsonization. This describes a form of Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID) or potentially X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) if light chains are also affected. Patients with antibody deficiencies typically present with recurrent infections, particularly with encapsulated bacteria (e.g., *Streptococcus pneumoniae*, *Haemophilus influenzae*), but not typically the specific pattern of catalase-positive organisms and granulomas seen in CGD. The DHR assay would be normal in antibody deficiencies.
- (B) Impaired T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the ZAP-70 kinase. This describes X-linked Ankyrin-1-binding domain 1 deficiency (X-linked immunodeficiency with magnesium deficiency, hypogammaglobulinemia, and endocrineopathy - IMD). Patients present with recurrent infections, often viral or opportunistic, and may have features like eczema, diabetes, and hypogammaglobulinemia. This does not fit the clinical picture of CGD, especially the specific infections and the DHR assay result.
- (C) Defective phagosome-lysosome fusion, resulting in inability to degrade ingested microbes. This describes Chediak-Higashi syndrome. Patients have recurrent pyogenic infections, partial oculocutaneous albinism, neurological abnormalities, and giant granules in leukocytes. While it involves phagocyte dysfunction, the underlying mechanism (defective lysosomal trafficking) and clinical features (albinism, giant granules) are distinct from CGD. The DHR assay might show some oxidative burst, although it can be impaired.
- (E) Defective complement component C3 convertase activity, leading to impaired bacterial lysis and opsonization. This describes deficiencies in complement components like C3 or Factor B/D, which are part of the alternative or classical pathway C3 convertases. Patients are susceptible to recurrent infections, particularly with encapsulated bacteria (like Neisseria species, which are poorly opsonized by alternative pathway C3b). This does not explain the specific catalase-positive infections, granulomas, or the absent oxidative burst seen in CGD.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID) | Chediak-Higashi Syndrome | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :------------------------------------- | :------------------------ | :-------------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH oxidase complex (ROS production) | B-cell differentiation/antibody production | Lysosomal trafficking     | B-cell development (pre-B cell)   |
| **Cell Type Affected**  | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | B-cells                               | Granulocytes, Melanocytes | B-cells                           |
| **Key Infections**      | Catalase-positive bacteria/fungi     | Encapsulated bacteria                  | Pyogenic bacteria        | Encapsulated bacteria             |
| **Hallmark Feature**    | Granulomas, Absent Oxidative Burst    | Hypogammaglobulinemia                  | Giant granules, Albinism  | Absent B-cells, Hypogammaglobulinemia |
| **Diagnostic Test**     | DHR assay (absent ROS)               | Serum Ig levels, B-cell counts         | Peripheral smear          | Serum Ig levels, B-cell counts    |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR           | Variable (often sporadic)              | Autosomal Recessive      | X-linked                          |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defective phagocyte oxidative burst due to mutations in NADPH oxidase subunits, leading to susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms and granuloma formation. The DHR assay is diagnostic. Differentiate CGD from other phagocyte defects like Chediak-Higashi syndrome (lysosomal trafficking defect, giant granules) and antibody deficiencies like CVID or XLA (recurrent encapsulated bacterial infections, low immunoglobulins).

<a id='question-090'></a>

### Question 090: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 40 (failure)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Defective phagocyte chemotaxis due to mutations in the *MYH9* gene, leading to impaired actin polymerization and inability to form lamellipodia, resulting in recurrent bacterial infections and impaired wound healing. (缺陷性吞噬细胞趋化性，由 *MYH9* 基因突变引起，导致肌动蛋白聚合受损，无法形成假足，引起复发性细菌感染和伤口愈合不良。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 2-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, lethargy, and a rapidly expanding, erythematous, warm, and tender lesion on his left lower leg. His mother reports that the lesion started as a small cut sustained during play in the sandbox two days prior. She notes that the wound has not healed normally and appears increasingly infected. The boy has a history of recurrent skin abscesses, omphalitis in the neonatal period, and delayed umbilical cord separation. He has also experienced recurrent respiratory infections, including pneumonia and otitis media. Physical examination reveals a high fever (39.5°C), tachycardia (140 bpm), and a large, purulent ulcer with surrounding cellulitis on the lower leg. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A skin biopsy from the edge of the ulcer reveals numerous neutrophils present in the tissue but lacking the characteristic formation of lamellipodia as they attempt to migrate towards the site of injury. Genetic testing confirms a homozygous mutation in the *MYH9* gene.

Which of the following cellular processes is most directly impaired in this patient, leading to his clinical presentation?

(A) Formation of the phagosome after engulfment of bacteria.
(B) Assembly of the NADPH oxidase complex for reactive oxygen species production.
(C) Polymerization of actin filaments required for cell motility and phagocytosis.
(D) Fusion of lysosomes with phagosomes to form the phagolysosome.
(E) Expression of CD18 on the surface of leukocytes, preventing adhesion to endothelial cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 2-year-old boy with recurrent skin abscesses, omphalitis, delayed cord separation, recurrent respiratory infections, and a rapidly worsening skin ulcer following minor trauma. This pattern strongly suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocyte function.
- **Specific Clue:** The skin biopsy shows neutrophils present in the tissue but *lacking lamellipodia formation*. Lamellipodia are crucial for cell migration, particularly for phagocytes moving towards sites of infection or injury (chemotaxis).
- **Genetic Confirmation:** Homozygous mutation in the *MYH9* gene. This gene encodes non-muscle myosin heavy chain IIA (NMMHC-IIA), a key component of the actin cytoskeleton essential for cell motility, phagocytosis, and intracellular transport in various cell types, including leukocytes.
- **Connecting Clues:** The clinical history (recurrent infections, impaired wound healing, delayed cord separation) points to a phagocyte defect. The biopsy finding (lack of lamellipodia) specifically implicates impaired cell motility. The *MYH9* mutation directly explains the defect in actin-based motility.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **MYH9 and Actin Cytoskeleton:** Non-muscle myosin heavy chain IIA (NMMHC-IIA), encoded by *MYH9*, is a motor protein that interacts with actin filaments. It plays a critical role in generating the contractile forces necessary for actin polymerization, cell shape changes, cell motility (including chemotaxis), phagocytosis (engulfment and movement), and intracellular transport.
- **Mechanism of Defect:** Mutations in *MYH9* lead to defective NMMHC-IIA function. This impairs the ability of phagocytes (like neutrophils) to polymerize actin filaments effectively at the leading edge of the cell. This failure prevents the formation of lamellipodia (thin, sheet-like protrusions) and filopodia (finger-like protrusions), which are essential structures for cell crawling and migration.
- **Clinical Consequences:**
    - **Impaired Chemotaxis:** Neutrophils cannot migrate effectively to sites of infection or injury, leading to recurrent bacterial infections (skin abscesses, pneumonia, otitis media) and impaired wound healing.
    - **Defective Phagocytosis:** Engulfment of pathogens may also be impaired due to defects in actin-dependent membrane remodeling.
    - **Delayed Cord Separation/Omphalitis:** Impaired neutrophil migration to the umbilical stump contributes to delayed separation and infection (omphalitis).
- **Differential Diagnosis:** This condition is known as Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome-like disorder or MYH9-related disorder. It shares some clinical features with Wiskott-Aldrich syndrome (WAS), but WAS is caused by mutations in the *WAS* gene, affecting actin polymerization through a different pathway (WASp protein). The key distinguishing feature here is the specific *MYH9* mutation and the direct impact on myosin-mediated actin dynamics.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Formation of the phagosome after engulfment of bacteria:** Phagosome formation involves membrane invagination and requires actin rearrangement, but the primary defect in *MYH9* mutations is the *motility* aspect (lamellipodia formation) due to impaired actin polymerization dynamics, not necessarily the initial engulfment process itself, although phagocytosis can be secondarily affected. This option describes a step *after* the initial migration failure highlighted in the vignette.
- (B) **Assembly of the NADPH oxidase complex for reactive oxygen species production:** Defects in NADPH oxidase assembly cause Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). Patients with CGD have recurrent bacterial and fungal infections, often forming granulomas, but their neutrophils *can* migrate and phagocytose; the defect is in the killing mechanism (lack of respiratory burst). The vignette specifically points to a motility defect (lack of lamellipodia).
- (D) **Fusion of lysosomes with phagosomes to form the phagolysosome:** Defects in phagolysosome fusion cause Chediak-Higashi syndrome. This is characterized by giant granules in leukocytes, oculocutaneous albinism, recurrent pyogenic infections, and neurological abnormalities. Neutrophil motility is generally intact in Chediak-Higashi syndrome. The vignette does not describe giant granules or albinism.
- (E) **Expression of CD18 on the surface of leukocytes, preventing adhesion to endothelial cells:** Defects in CD18 (part of the β2 integrin family) cause Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 1 (LAD-1). Patients with LAD-1 have impaired leukocyte adhesion to endothelium and extravasation, leading to recurrent infections, delayed umbilical cord separation, and impaired wound healing. However, the key defect is adhesion/extravasation, not *migration within the tissue* after extravasation. While there might be some overlap in symptoms, the specific biopsy finding of neutrophils lacking lamellipodia points directly to an intracellular motility defect, not an adhesion defect. Also, LAD-1 typically presents with *very high* neutrophil counts in the blood because they cannot exit the vasculature effectively.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | MYH9-Related Disorder (Wiskott-Aldrich-like) | CGD (NADPH Oxidase Defect) | Chediak-Higashi (Lysosome Fusion Defect) | LAD-1 (CD18 Defect) |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------------ | :------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :------------------ |
| **Primary Defect**      | Actin polymerization/cell motility           | Respiratory burst (ROS)    | Lysosome trafficking/fusion              | Leukocyte adhesion  |
| **Gene Affected**       | *MYH9*                                      | *CYBB*, *NCF1*, etc.       | *LYST*                                   | *ITGB2*             |
| **Key Cell Function**   | Chemotaxis, phagocytosis motility           | Killing of phagocytosed microbes | Phagolysosome formation                  | Extravasation       |
| **Hallmark Finding**    | Lack of lamellipodia                         | Granulomas                 | Giant granules, albinism                  | High circulating neutrophils |
| **Clinical Features**   | Recurrent infections, poor wound healing, delayed cord separation | Recurrent infections, granulomas | Recurrent infections, albinism, neurological issues | Recurrent infections, delayed cord separation, poor wound healing |
| **Neutrophil Motility** | Impaired                                    | Normal                     | Normal                                   | Normal (after extravasation) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The vignette describes a primary immunodeficiency characterized by impaired neutrophil chemotaxis and phagocytosis due to defective actin polymerization, specifically linked to a *MYH9* mutation. This results in recurrent bacterial infections and poor wound healing. Key differentials include CGD (impaired killing), Chediak-Higashi (impaired lysosomal fusion), and LAD-1 (impaired adhesion/extravasation), each with distinct underlying mechanisms and clinical/pathological features. Understanding the specific role of actin in phagocyte function is crucial for differentiating these disorders.
