<a id='question-091'></a>

### Question 091: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 41 (delayed)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular defect in Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD), specifically the impaired function of NADPH oxidase, leading to defective phagosome-lysosome fusion and inability to generate reactive oxygen species (ROS) necessary for killing certain intracellular pathogens. (慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 的分子缺陷，即 NADPH 氧化酶功能障碍，导致吞噬体-溶酶体融合受损，无法产生杀死某些胞内病原体所需的活性氧 (ROS)。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of fever, cough, and shortness of breath. He has a history of recurrent pneumonia, skin abscesses, and lymphadenitis since infancy, often requiring hospitalization. His mother reports that his infections are typically caused by organisms like *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, and *Aspergillus* species. Family history is significant for a similarly affected maternal uncle who died in his 20s from overwhelming sepsis. Physical examination reveals a cachectic boy with multiple draining skin lesions and cervical lymphadenopathy. Chest X-ray shows bilateral pulmonary infiltrates and cavitation. A sputum sample is sent for culture and analysis. Flow cytometry analysis of neutrophils shows normal expression of CD11b/CD18 (LFA-1) and CD16 (FcγRIII), but markedly reduced dihydrorhodamine (DHR) oxidation upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA). Which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for this patient's condition?

(A) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) shared by IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21 receptors.
(B) Defective expression of CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T cells.
(C) Defective assembly or function of the NADPH oxidase complex in phagocytes.
(D) Defective expression of the CD154 (CD40L) receptor on T cells.
(E) Defective expression of the Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) enzyme in B cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 6-year-old boy with recurrent, severe infections (pneumonia, skin abscesses, lymphadenitis) since infancy. This pattern suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function.
- **Infecting Organisms:** *S. aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *B. cepacia*, *Aspergillus*. These are catalase-positive organisms (bacteria and fungi) that are typically killed by phagocytes via oxidative burst. The susceptibility to these specific organisms is a hallmark of CGD.
- **Family History:** Affected maternal uncle suggests an X-linked inheritance pattern, common in CGD.
- **Physical Exam:** Cachexia, draining skin lesions, lymphadenopathy, pulmonary infiltrates/cavitation are consistent with chronic, severe infections and granuloma formation (though granulomas are not explicitly mentioned, the severe infections and specific pathogens point towards this).
- **Flow Cytometry:** Normal CD11b/CD18 (LFA-1) and CD16 (FcγRIII) expression rules out Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD-1) and FcγRIII deficiency, respectively. Markedly reduced DHR oxidation upon PMA stimulation is the key diagnostic finding for CGD. PMA activates protein kinase C, which normally triggers the respiratory burst via NADPH oxidase. Failure of DHR oxidation indicates a defect in ROS production.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying molecular defect responsible for the condition.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** An inherited primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase enzyme complex.
- **NADPH Oxidase Function:** This multi-subunit enzyme complex is located in the phagosomal membrane of phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages). Its function is to catalyze the reduction of molecular oxygen (O2) to superoxide anion (O2•−) using NADPH as an electron donor. Superoxide is then converted to other reactive oxygen species (ROS), such as hydrogen peroxide (H2O2), hydroxyl radical (•OH), and hypochlorous acid (HOCl), which are crucial for killing ingested microbes, especially catalase-positive bacteria and fungi.
- **Molecular Defect in CGD:** Mutations in genes encoding subunits of the NADPH oxidase complex (e.g., *CYBB* encoding gp91phox, *CYBA* encoding p22phox, *NCF1* encoding p47phox, *NCF2* encoding p67phox, *NCF4* encoding p40phox). The most common form is X-linked (gp91phox defect), followed by autosomal recessive forms.
- **Pathophysiology:** Without functional NADPH oxidase, phagocytes cannot generate ROS. This leads to impaired killing of catalase-positive organisms, resulting in recurrent infections and granuloma formation (attempted containment of pathogens).
- **DHR Assay:** Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) is a non-fluorescent dye that is oxidized to fluorescent rhodamine by ROS generated during the respiratory burst. In CGD, phagocytes fail to produce ROS, so DHR remains non-fluorescent, indicating the defect.
- **Differential Diagnosis & Immune Pathways:**
    - Option A (γc deficiency): Leads to X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID), affecting T, B, and NK cell development due to impaired cytokine signaling. Patients present early with severe opportunistic infections (viral, fungal, bacterial).
    - Option B (CD40L deficiency): Leads to X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. T cells cannot provide help to B cells for class switching and affinity maturation. Patients have recurrent bacterial infections (especially encapsulated organisms) and opportunistic infections (e.g., *Pneumocystis jirovecii*).
    - Option D (CD40 receptor deficiency): Leads to autosomal recessive Hyper-IgM syndrome, similar clinical presentation to CD40L deficiency.
    - Option E (BTK deficiency): Leads to X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA). B cell development is arrested, resulting in absent B cells and immunoglobulins. Patients have recurrent bacterial infections, especially encapsulated organisms, starting after maternal antibodies wane.

##### 3. Linking the Vignette to the Correct Answer
The patient's clinical presentation (recurrent infections with specific catalase-positive organisms), family history (suggesting X-linked inheritance), and the key diagnostic finding (impaired DHR oxidation) strongly point towards Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). CGD is caused by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, which is responsible for generating ROS. Therefore, the most likely molecular defect is (C).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc): This causes X-linked SCID. Patients with SCID typically present earlier with severe infections involving viruses, fungi, and intracellular bacteria, due to profound T, B, and NK cell deficiency. The specific pathogens (*S. aureus*, *Serratia*, *Aspergillus*) and the DHR assay result are inconsistent with SCID.
- (B) Defective expression of CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T cells: This causes X-linked Hyper-IgM syndrome. Patients have impaired class switching and affinity maturation, leading to high IgM and low IgG/IgA/IgE. They are susceptible to bacterial and opportunistic infections, but the hallmark is not impaired phagocyte killing of catalase-positive organisms, and the DHR assay would be normal.
- (D) Defective expression of the CD154 (CD40L) receptor on T cells: This is incorrect terminology; CD154 is the name of the ligand (CD40L), and the receptor is CD40, expressed on B cells, macrophages, and dendritic cells. Defective CD40 receptor expression (autosomal recessive Hyper-IgM syndrome) has a similar clinical presentation to CD40L deficiency but is caused by a different genetic defect. Neither form of Hyper-IgM syndrome involves defective ROS production.
- (E) Defective expression of the Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) enzyme in B cells: This causes X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA). Patients lack mature B cells and immunoglobulins, leading to recurrent bacterial infections, primarily with encapsulated organisms. Phagocyte function (including ROS production) is normal, and the DHR assay would be normal.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | X-linked SCID (γc deficiency) | X-linked Hyper-IgM (CD40L deficiency) | XLA (BTK deficiency) |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH oxidase function              | γc signaling                 | CD40L expression                     | BTK expression       |
| **Affected Cells**      | Phagocytes (Neutrophils, Macrophages) | T, B, NK cells               | T cells (help for B cells)           | B cells              |
| **Key Pathogen Susceptibility** | Catalase+ bacteria, fungi        | Viruses, fungi, intracellular bacteria | Encapsulated bacteria, *Pneumocystis* | Encapsulated bacteria |
| **Typical Infections**  | Pneumonia, abscesses, granulomas    | Severe, early, opportunistic | Recurrent bacterial, opportunistic   | Recurrent bacterial  |
| **DHR Assay**           | Decreased/Absent fluorescence        | Normal fluorescence          | Normal fluorescence                  | Normal fluorescence  |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR          | X-linked                    | X-linked                             | X-linked             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocyte function due to defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired ROS production and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. The DHR assay is diagnostic. Key differentials include other phagocyte defects (e.g., LAD), antibody deficiencies (e.g., XLA, Hyper-IgM), and T cell deficiencies (e.g., SCID). Remember the specific pathogens associated with CGD (*Staph*, *Serratia*, *Burkholderia*, *Aspergillus*).

<a id='question-092'></a>

### Question 092: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 42 (separation)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Defective leukocyte adhesion (黏附缺陷) due to mutations in integrin subunits (整合素亚基突变) leads to impaired leukocyte migration (白细胞迁移受损) to sites of infection, resulting in recurrent bacterial infections (复发性细菌感染), delayed umbilical cord separation (脐带脱落延迟), and impaired wound healing (伤口愈合障碍). (Defective leukocyte adhesion / 黏附缺陷; Integrin mutations / 整合素亚基突变; Impaired leukocyte migration / 白细胞迁移受损; Recurrent bacterial infections / 复发性细菌感染; Delayed umbilical cord separation / 脐带脱落延迟; Impaired wound healing / 伤口愈合障碍)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 2-day history of fever, lethargy, and a rapidly spreading cellulitis around a minor abrasion on his left knee sustained during play. His mother reports that his umbilical cord did not fall off until 4 weeks after birth (normal is typically within 1-2 weeks). He has a history of recurrent, severe bacterial infections, including pneumonia and otitis media, requiring frequent hospitalizations. Physical examination reveals a high fever (39.5°C), tachycardia (140 bpm), and a markedly erythematous, warm, and edematous area surrounding the abrasion, with purulent discharge. Laboratory tests show marked leukocytosis (35,000/µL) with a neutrophilic predominance, but the neutrophils fail to migrate effectively to the site of infection. A peripheral blood smear shows normal morphology of leukocytes, but flow cytometry reveals absent expression of CD18 on neutrophils and monocytes.

(A) Deficiency in the complement component C3, leading to impaired opsonization and bacterial killing.
(B) Defective phagosome-lysosome fusion due to mutations in the *LYST* gene, resulting in impaired intracellular killing of phagocytosed bacteria.
(C) Impaired T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the *ZAP70* gene, leading to defective T-cell activation and impaired cell-mediated immunity.
(D) Defective expression of integrin subunits, specifically CD18, leading to impaired leukocyte adhesion and migration.
(E) Defective production of immunoglobulins due to mutations in the *BTK* gene, resulting in recurrent sinopulmonary infections with encapsulated bacteria.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Age and Presentation:** A 3-year-old boy with recurrent severe bacterial infections (pneumonia, otitis media, cellulitis) points towards an underlying immunodeficiency.
- **Delayed Umbilical Cord Separation:** Separation occurring at 4 weeks is significantly delayed (normal < 2 weeks). This is a classic hallmark of Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD).
- **Recurrent Severe Bacterial Infections:** Frequent infections, especially bacterial ones, are characteristic of defects in innate immunity, particularly phagocyte function.
- **Cellulitis with Purulent Discharge:** Indicates a bacterial infection involving skin and subcutaneous tissues, often seen in phagocyte defects.
- **Marked Leukocytosis with Neutrophilic Predominance:** The body is producing white blood cells in response to infection, but they are not effectively reaching the site.
- **Failure of Neutrophil Migration:** This is the key functional defect. Despite high neutrophil counts in the blood, they cannot extravasate and migrate to the infected tissue.
- **Flow Cytometry: Absent CD18:** CD18 is the common beta-2 integrin subunit (β2 integrin). Its absence confirms the diagnosis of LAD type 1.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD):** This is a group of primary immunodeficiencies characterized by defects in leukocyte adhesion and migration.
- **LAD Type 1 (LAD-I):** The most common form, caused by mutations in the *ITGB2* gene, which encodes the CD18 subunit common to several β2 integrins (LFA-1, Mac-1, p150,95). These integrins are crucial for leukocyte adhesion to endothelial cells (via ICAM-1) and subsequent extravasation into tissues. Without functional CD18, neutrophils cannot adhere firmly to the endothelium or migrate out of blood vessels to fight infection.
- **Pathophysiology:** The absence of CD18 prevents leukocytes from binding to endothelial ligands (like ICAM-1) expressed during inflammation. This impairs rolling, firm adhesion, and transmigration across the endothelium.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** Recurrent bacterial infections (skin, mucosal surfaces, respiratory tract) without pus formation (due to impaired neutrophil migration), delayed umbilical cord separation (>3 weeks), impaired wound healing, and often severe periodontitis.
- **Diagnosis:** Clinical suspicion based on history (delayed cord separation, recurrent infections) and confirmed by flow cytometry showing absent or reduced CD18 expression on leukocytes.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Deficiency in the complement component C3:** C3 deficiency impairs opsonization (C3b) and formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC). This leads to increased susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria (e.g., *Neisseria meningitidis*, *Streptococcus pneumoniae*, *Haemophilus influenzae*). While it causes recurrent bacterial infections, it does *not* cause delayed umbilical cord separation or impaired wound healing, nor does it directly affect leukocyte migration. The key feature here is the *failure of migration* despite high neutrophil counts, which is not explained by C3 deficiency.
- (B) **Defective phagosome-lysosome fusion due to mutations in the *LYST* gene:** This describes Chediak-Higashi syndrome. It results in giant granules in leukocytes and other cells, impaired chemotaxis, and defective intracellular killing. While it causes recurrent bacterial infections, it is characterized by oculocutaneous albinism, neurological abnormalities, and giant granules on blood smear, none of which are described in the vignette. Delayed cord separation is not a typical feature.
- (C) **Impaired T-cell receptor signaling due to mutations in the *ZAP70* gene:** This describes ZAP70 deficiency, a form of severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID). It affects T-cell and NK-cell development and function, leading to susceptibility to a wide range of pathogens (bacterial, viral, fungal, opportunistic). It does *not* cause delayed umbilical cord separation or specifically impair neutrophil migration. Patients typically present earlier with severe, life-threatening infections.
- (E) **Defective production of immunoglobulins due to mutations in the *BTK* gene:** This describes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA). It affects B-cell development, leading to a lack of mature B cells and immunoglobulins. Patients suffer from recurrent infections primarily with encapsulated bacteria, starting after maternal antibodies wane (around 6 months). It does *not* affect leukocyte adhesion, migration, or cause delayed umbilical cord separation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | LAD Type 1 (Correct Answer) | C3 Deficiency (A) | Chediak-Higashi (B) | ZAP70 Deficiency (C) | XLA (E) |
|-----------------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------|----------------------|----------------------|---------|
| **Primary Defect**          | CD18 (β2 integrin)          | C3                 | LYST (phagosome fusion) | ZAP70 (TCR signaling) | BTK (B cell development) |
| **Cell Type Affected**      | Neutrophils, Monocytes      | All cells          | Neutrophils, etc.    | T cells, NK cells    | B cells |
| **Key Clinical Feature**    | Delayed cord separation     | N/A                | Giant granules, Albinism | Severe early infections | Recurrent sinopulmonary infections |
| **Neutrophil Migration**    | Impaired                    | Normal             | Impaired             | Normal               | Normal  |
| **Pus Formation**           | Absent/Poor                 | Normal             | Normal               | Normal               | Normal  |
| **Typical Infections**      | Bacterial (skin, mucosa)    | Encapsulated bacteria | Bacterial            | Broad range (viral, fungal, bacterial) | Encapsulated bacteria |
| **Diagnostic Marker**       | Absent CD18                 | Low C3             | Giant granules        | Absent T/NK cells    | Absent B cells/Ig |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD) type 1 is characterized by defective leukocyte adhesion and migration due to mutations in the CD18 integrin subunit, leading to delayed umbilical cord separation, impaired wound healing, and recurrent bacterial infections without pus formation. Differentiating LAD from other primary immunodeficiencies requires recognizing these specific clinical hallmarks and understanding the underlying cellular defect. Remember to consider defects in phagocyte function (LAD, Chediak-Higashi), complement (C3 deficiency), T-cell function (SCID), and B-cell function (XLA) when evaluating recurrent infections.

<a id='question-093'></a>

### Question 093: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 43 (umbilical)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular defect in Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) deficiency, a type of Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID), and its consequences on lymphocyte development and function, specifically purine metabolism and lymphocyte toxicity. (考察腺苷脱氨酶 (ADA) 缺乏症的分子缺陷，一种重症联合免疫缺陷症 (SCID) 的类型，及其对淋巴细胞发育和功能的影响，特别是嘌呤代谢和淋巴细胞毒性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents with recurrent severe infections, including pneumonia, otitis media, and oral thrush, starting shortly after birth. He has failed to thrive and exhibits chronic diarrhea. Family history reveals that his older sister died at 1 year of age from an unknown cause, also suffering from recurrent infections. Physical examination shows generalized lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory studies reveal profound lymphopenia (absolute lymphocyte count < 500/µL) with markedly reduced T cells (CD3+ < 100/µL) and B cells (CD19+ < 50/µL), and absent NK cells (CD16+/CD56+). Flow cytometry confirms the absence of T-cell receptor (TCR) excision circles (TRECs) in peripheral blood lymphocytes. A bone marrow biopsy shows markedly reduced cellularity with a paucity of lymphoid precursors. Given the clinical presentation and laboratory findings, which of the following molecular defects is most likely responsible for this patient's immunodeficiency?

(A) Defective recombination activating gene 2 (RAG2) leading to impaired V(D)J recombination.
(B) Defective common gamma chain (γc) subunit of cytokine receptors, impairing signal transduction.
(C) Defective adenosine deaminase (ADA) leading to accumulation of toxic purine metabolites.
(D) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) leading to impaired B-cell development.
(E) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L) leading to impaired T-cell help for B-cell activation.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Age and Presentation:** 6-month-old infant with recurrent, severe infections (pneumonia, otitis media, thrush) starting early in life, failure to thrive, chronic diarrhea. This points towards a severe primary immunodeficiency affecting multiple immune cell types.
- **Family History:** Older sister died young from recurrent infections, suggesting a genetic, potentially autosomal recessive or X-linked disorder.
- **Physical Exam:** Generalized lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly can be seen in various immunodeficiencies, including SCID, often due to chronic infections or infiltration.
- **Laboratory Findings:**
    - **Profound Lymphopenia:** Low absolute lymphocyte count (< 500/µL).
    - **Reduced T cells (CD3+ < 100/µL):** Indicates severe T-cell deficiency.
    - **Reduced B cells (CD19+ < 50/µL):** Indicates severe B-cell deficiency.
    - **Absent NK cells (CD16+/CD56+):** Indicates severe NK-cell deficiency.
    - **Absent TRECs:** T-cell receptor excision circles are byproducts of V(D)J recombination in the thymus. Their absence indicates a failure of T-cell development in the thymus.
    - **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Reduced cellularity and paucity of lymphoid precursors confirm a defect in lymphocyte production or survival.
- **Overall Picture:** The combination of severe deficiency in T cells, B cells, and NK cells, along with absent TRECs and a history suggestive of early-onset SCID, strongly points towards a defect affecting lymphocyte development or survival. The question asks for the *molecular defect*.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (SCID):** SCID is a group of genetic disorders characterized by profound defects in T-cell development, often accompanied by defects in B-cell and/or NK-cell development. This leads to extreme susceptibility to infections early in life.
- **Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) Deficiency:** ADA is an enzyme crucial for purine metabolism. It catalyzes the deamination of adenosine and deoxyadenosine to inosine and deoxyinosine, respectively. Deficiency of ADA, typically autosomal recessive, leads to the accumulation of deoxyadenosine and its phosphorylated metabolites (dATP) within lymphocytes, particularly T and B cells. These metabolites are toxic, inhibiting ribonucleotide reductase, an enzyme essential for DNA synthesis, thereby blocking lymphocyte proliferation and inducing apoptosis. This results in severe lymphopenia affecting T, B, and NK cells, fitting the clinical picture. The absence of TRECs is consistent with a failure of T-cell development due to this toxicity.
- **Other SCID Types:**
    - **RAG2 Deficiency (A):** RAG1 and RAG2 enzymes are essential for V(D)J recombination, the process that generates diverse T-cell receptors (TCRs) and B-cell receptors (BCRs). Deficiency leads to a lack of functional T and B cells, but NK cells are typically normal. TRECs would be absent.
    - **Common Gamma Chain (γc) Deficiency (B):** The γc subunit is shared by receptors for IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. IL-7 is critical for T-cell and NK-cell development, while IL-15 is important for NK-cell development. Deficiency leads to absent or severely reduced T cells and NK cells, but B-cell numbers are usually normal (though function is impaired due to lack of T-cell help). This is the most common form of SCID and is X-linked.
    - **Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase (BTK) Deficiency (D):** BTK is essential for B-cell development, specifically the transition from pro-B to pre-B cells. Deficiency leads to a block in B-cell maturation, resulting in absent mature B cells and immunoglobulins, but T-cell and NK-cell numbers are normal. This causes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA), not SCID.
    - **CD40 Ligand (CD40L) Deficiency (E):** CD40L (CD154) on activated T cells binds to CD40 on B cells, antigen-presenting cells (APCs), and other cells, providing crucial co-stimulation for B-cell activation, isotype switching, and germinal center formation. Deficiency leads to impaired B-cell function (hyper-IgM syndrome) and defective cell-mediated immunity, but T-cell numbers are usually normal.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Defective RAG2: This causes SCID by impairing V(D)J recombination, leading to absent T and B cells. However, NK cells are typically normal in RAG deficiencies. The vignette describes absent NK cells, making this less likely than ADA deficiency, which affects all three lineages.
- (B) Defective common gamma chain (γc): This is the most common cause of SCID (X-linked SCID). It affects T-cell and NK-cell development (due to lack of IL-7/IL-15 signaling) but typically spares B-cell numbers (though function is impaired). The vignette describes absent B cells, making this less likely than ADA deficiency.
- (D) Defective Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK): This causes X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA), characterized by a lack of mature B cells and immunoglobulins, but normal T-cell and NK-cell numbers and function. The vignette describes severe defects in T, B, and NK cells, ruling out BTK deficiency.
- (E) Defective CD40 ligand (CD40L): This causes X-linked hyper-IgM syndrome. T-cell numbers are usually normal, but B-cell function (isotype switching, germinal center formation) is impaired, leading to high IgM and low IgG/IgA/IgE. The vignette describes severe defects in T, B, and NK cells, ruling out CD40L deficiency.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | ADA Deficiency (C) | RAG2 Deficiency (A) | γc Deficiency (B) | BTK Deficiency (D) | CD40L Deficiency (E) |
| :---------------------- | :---------------- | :---------------- | :--------------- | :---------------- | :------------------- |
| **Inheritance**         | AR                | AR                | X-linked         | X-linked          | X-linked            |
| **T Cells**             | Absent/↓          | Absent            | Absent/↓        | Normal            | Normal              |
| **B Cells**             | Absent/↓          | Absent            | Normal           | Absent            | Normal (function ↓)  |
| **NK Cells**            | Absent/↓          | Normal            | Absent/↓        | Normal            | Normal              |
| **TRECs**               | Absent            | Absent            | Absent           | Present           | Present             |
| **Mechanism**           | Purine toxicity   | V(D)J block       | Cytokine signal block | B-cell maturation block | B-cell activation block |
| **Key Lab Finding**     | ↑ dATP, ↓ ADA act | None specific     | None specific    | ↓ Ig, ↓ B cells   | ↑ IgM, ↓ IgG/IgA/IgE |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
ADA deficiency is a cause of SCID characterized by severe lymphopenia affecting T, B, and NK cells due to the accumulation of toxic purine metabolites. Differentiating SCID types requires careful analysis of which lymphocyte lineages are affected. Remember that RAG deficiencies spare NK cells, γc deficiency spares B cells, BTK deficiency affects only B cells, and CD40L deficiency affects only B-cell function (not numbers). The absence of TRECs points to a defect in T-cell development.

<a id='question-094'></a>

### Question 094: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 44 (Recurrent)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Defective intracellular signaling pathway leading to impaired phagocyte function and recurrent infections, specifically focusing on the role of Syk kinase in B-cell and phagocyte signaling. (缺陷的细胞内信号通路导致吞噬功能障碍和复发性感染，具体关注Syk激酶在B细胞和吞噬细胞信号传导中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with a history of recurrent, severe bacterial infections starting shortly after birth. These infections include pneumonia (twice), otitis media (four times), sinusitis (three times), and a life-threatening case of *Staphylococcus aureus* sepsis at 6 months of age. He has also experienced persistent diarrhea and failure to thrive. Physical examination reveals splenomegaly and lymphadenopathy. Laboratory investigations show markedly elevated levels of IgM (250 mg/dL; normal < 80 mg/dL) and significantly reduced levels of IgG (200 mg/dL; normal > 400 mg/dL) and IgA (< 10 mg/dL; normal > 50 mg/dL). Peripheral blood smear shows normal numbers of B cells, but flow cytometry reveals absent expression of CD20 on B cells. Furthermore, his B cells fail to proliferate in response to stimulation with anti-IgM antibodies. His phagocytes (neutrophils and macrophages) exhibit impaired chemotaxis and reduced ability to kill ingested bacteria, despite normal numbers and morphology. Genetic testing reveals a homozygous mutation in the *Spleen tyrosine kinase* (Syk) gene.

Which of the following intracellular signaling pathways is most directly impaired in this patient, leading to his clinical presentation?

(A) JAK-STAT pathway involved in cytokine receptor signaling.
(B) PI3K-Akt pathway involved in cell survival and growth.
(C) Syk-dependent pathway involved in B-cell receptor and Fc receptor signaling.
(D) NF-κB pathway involved in inflammatory cytokine production.
(E) MAP kinase pathway involved in T-cell receptor signaling.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe bacterial infections:** Suggests a defect in humoral immunity (antibody production) or phagocyte function. The specific pathogens (*S. aureus*, common respiratory bacteria) point towards opsonization defects or impaired phagocytosis/killing.
- **Elevated IgM, low IgG/IgA:** This pattern (hyper-IgM syndrome) indicates a defect in immunoglobulin class switching, which normally occurs in germinal centers with T-cell help.
- **Normal B cell numbers, absent CD20, failed proliferation to anti-IgM:** Normal B cell numbers rule out severe B cell lymphopenia (like XLA). Absent CD20 is unusual but can occur in certain B cell disorders. Failure to proliferate to anti-IgM suggests a defect in B-cell receptor (BCR) signaling.
- **Impaired phagocyte chemotaxis and killing:** Points to a defect in phagocyte function, potentially linked to the B cell defect, as some signaling pathways are shared.
- **Homozygous mutation in *Syk* gene:** This is the definitive clue. Syk (Spleen tyrosine kinase) is a crucial non-receptor tyrosine kinase involved in signaling downstream of various receptors, including the BCR, Fc receptors, and certain cytokine receptors.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Syk Kinase:** Syk is a cytoplasmic tyrosine kinase essential for signal transduction in hematopoietic cells. It is activated by phosphorylation (often by Src family kinases) and then phosphorylates downstream targets like BLNK (B-cell linker), leading to activation of transcription factors and other signaling molecules.
- **BCR Signaling:** Upon antigen binding to the BCR, Src family kinases phosphorylate immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motifs (ITAMs) on the CD79b/Igα/Igβ complex. Syk binds to these phosphorylated ITAMs and is activated. Activated Syk then phosphorylates downstream targets, initiating B-cell activation, proliferation, and differentiation, including class switch recombination (CSR) and somatic hypermutation (SHM).
- **Fc Receptor Signaling:** Syk is also crucial for signaling downstream of Fc receptors (e.g., FcγRIIB) on B cells, macrophages, and neutrophils. This signaling is important for antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC), phagocytosis, and inflammatory responses.
- **Hyper-IgM Syndrome:** Defects in Syk or its downstream targets (like CD40L on T cells, or CD40 on B cells) lead to impaired CSR and affinity maturation, resulting in high levels of IgM (the first antibody produced) and low levels of other isotypes (IgG, IgA, IgE).
- **Phagocyte Dysfunction:** Syk is involved in signaling downstream of Fc receptors on phagocytes. Defective Syk signaling impairs Fc receptor-mediated phagocytosis and intracellular killing of opsonized bacteria.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The patient's presentation (recurrent bacterial infections, hyper-IgM syndrome, impaired phagocyte function) and the specific *Syk* gene mutation directly implicate a defect in the Syk-dependent signaling pathway. This pathway is essential for both B-cell development/function (including CSR) and phagocyte activation via Fc receptors.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **JAK-STAT pathway:** This pathway is primarily involved in signaling downstream of cytokine receptors (e.g., interferons, interleukins, growth factors). While important for immune cell development and function, defects typically cause different syndromes (e.g., STAT3 deficiency leading to hyper-IgE syndrome, JAK3 deficiency leading to X-linked SCID). It is not the primary pathway affected by Syk mutations.
- (B) **PI3K-Akt pathway:** This pathway is involved in cell survival, growth, proliferation, and metabolism. While it plays a role in immune cell function, defects are not typically associated with the specific combination of hyper-IgM syndrome and impaired phagocytosis seen here. For example, mutations in *PIK3CD* can cause immunodeficiency, but the phenotype is different.
- (D) **NF-κB pathway:** This pathway is crucial for regulating the expression of genes involved in inflammation, immunity, and cell survival. It is activated by various stimuli, including TLRs, cytokines, and BCR signaling (downstream of Syk). While Syk can activate NF-κB, the primary defect in Syk deficiency is upstream of NF-κB activation in BCR and FcR signaling. Defects in NF-κB pathway components (e.g., NEMO in X-linked ectodermal dysplasia and immunodeficiency) cause distinct syndromes.
- (E) **MAP kinase pathway:** This pathway (including ERK, JNK, p38) is involved in cell proliferation, differentiation, and stress responses. It is activated downstream of T-cell receptors and B-cell receptors, but Syk is upstream of MAP kinase activation in BCR signaling. Defects in MAP kinase pathway components are associated with different immunodeficiencies or inflammatory conditions.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Syk Deficiency (Hyper-IgM 2) | JAK-STAT Deficiency (e.g., Hyper-IgE) | PI3K-Akt Deficiency | NF-κB Deficiency (e.g., NEMO) | MAP Kinase Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------- | :------------------------------------- | :----------------- | :---------------------------- | :-------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | Syk Kinase                  | JAK Kinase (e.g., JAK3, TYK2)           | PI3K Subunit      | NF-κB Subunit (e.g., NEMO)     | MAP Kinase Component |
| **Ig Levels**           | High IgM, Low IgG/IgA/IgE   | High IgE, Variable IgG/IgA             | Variable           | Variable                     | Variable             |
| **B Cell Function**     | Defective CSR, Proliferation | Normal CSR                            | Variable           | Variable                     | Variable             |
| **Phagocyte Function**  | Impaired (FcR signaling)    | Normal                                 | Variable           | Variable                     | Variable             |
| **Key Infections**      | Encapsulated bacteria       | *Staph. aureus* (skin/lungs), Viruses   | Variable           | Variable                     | Variable             |
| **Other Features**      | Splenomegaly, Lymphadenopathy | Eczema, Skeletal abnormalities         | Variable           | Ectodermal dysplasia          | Variable             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Syk kinase is essential for signaling downstream of the B-cell receptor (BCR) and Fc receptors. Mutations in *Syk* lead to impaired B-cell activation, defective immunoglobulin class switching (resulting in hyper-IgM syndrome), and impaired phagocyte function via Fc receptors. This contrasts with other primary immunodeficiencies affecting different signaling pathways (JAK-STAT, PI3K-Akt, NF-κB, MAP kinase) which have distinct clinical and laboratory features. Remember the key role of Syk in both B-cell and phagocyte signaling.

<a id='question-095'></a>

### Question 095: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 45 (infection)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte oxidative burst and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (慢性肉芽肿病 (CGD) 的特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合物的缺陷，导致吞噬细胞氧化爆发受损，并易感于 catalase 阳性生物。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 10-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of recurrent pneumonia, abdominal pain, and weight loss. He has had multiple hospitalizations in the past year for similar infections, including pneumonia, skin abscesses, and osteomyelitis, often caused by *Staphylococcus aureus* and *Aspergillus* species. His family history is significant for a maternal uncle who died from complications of tuberculosis at age 15. Physical examination reveals a cachectic male child with multiple draining skin lesions and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A nitroblue tetrazolium (NBT) test is performed on his neutrophils, which fail to turn blue upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA). Flow cytometry reveals normal expression of CD14 and CD16 on neutrophils. Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective synthesis of superoxide dismutase (SOD)
(B) Defective synthesis of catalase
(C) Defective synthesis of the NADPH oxidase complex subunits
(D) Defective synthesis of the complement component C5
(E) Defective synthesis of the Fc receptor gamma chain (Fcγc)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 10-year-old boy with recurrent, severe infections (pneumonia, skin abscesses, osteomyelitis, abdominal pain, weight loss). This pattern suggests an underlying immunodeficiency, particularly affecting phagocytes.
- **Infecting Organisms:** *Staphylococcus aureus* and *Aspergillus* species are common pathogens in CGD. These organisms are catalase-positive, meaning they produce catalase, an enzyme that breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2).
- **Family History:** Maternal uncle dying young from tuberculosis suggests an X-linked inheritance pattern, common for CGD (specifically the most common form).
- **Physical Exam:** Cachexia, draining skin lesions, hepatosplenomegaly are consistent with chronic, severe infections and inflammation.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukocytosis with neutrophilia indicates an active inflammatory response, but the neutrophils are functionally impaired.
- **Nitroblue Tetrazolium (NBT) Test:** Failure of neutrophils to reduce NBT (turn blue) upon PMA stimulation indicates a defect in the respiratory burst, the process by which phagocytes generate reactive oxygen species (ROS) to kill ingested microbes. PMA directly activates protein kinase C, which phosphorylates and activates NADPH oxidase.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Normal CD14 and CD16 expression rules out defects in neutrophil surface markers or adhesion molecules, focusing the defect on intracellular function.
- **Synthesis:** The constellation of recurrent catalase-positive bacterial and fungal infections, family history suggestive of X-linked inheritance, and a defective NBT test strongly points towards Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). CGD is caused by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, which is responsible for generating superoxide radicals (O2-) during the respiratory burst.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Phagocyte Killing Mechanisms:** Phagocytes (neutrophils, macrophages) engulf pathogens (phagocytosis). Killing occurs primarily via two mechanisms:
    1.  **Oxygen-dependent killing:** Involves the respiratory burst, where NADPH oxidase generates superoxide (O2-), which is converted to hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) by superoxide dismutase (SOD). H2O2 is then used by myeloperoxidase (MPO) to form hypochlorous acid (HOCl, bleach), a potent antimicrobial agent.
    2.  **Oxygen-independent killing:** Involves enzymes like lysozyme, lactoferrin, defensins, and low pH within the phagolysosome.
- **NADPH Oxidase Complex:** This multi-subunit enzyme complex (gp91phox, p22phox, p47phox, p67phox, p40phox) is located in the phagosomal membrane. Upon activation (e.g., by PMA or microbial components), its subunits assemble to transfer electrons from NADPH to molecular oxygen (O2), generating superoxide (O2-).
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** Genetic defects in any of the NADPH oxidase subunits (most commonly *CYBB* encoding gp91phox, which is X-linked) prevent the generation of superoxide and downstream ROS. This impairs the killing of catalase-positive organisms (*S. aureus*, *Aspergillus*, *Serratia*, *Burkholderia*, *Nocardia*). Catalase breaks down H2O2, preventing its accumulation and subsequent use by MPO. Therefore, organisms that produce catalase are particularly problematic in CGD because the primary ROS-generating pathway is blocked, and the H2O2 produced is rapidly degraded.
- **NBT Test:** This test assesses the ability of neutrophils to produce superoxide. NBT is a yellow dye that is reduced to blue formazan by superoxide. In normal neutrophils, PMA stimulation activates NADPH oxidase, leading to superoxide production and blue formazan deposition. In CGD neutrophils, the NADPH oxidase is defective, so superoxide is not produced, and the NBT remains yellow.
- **Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) Flow Cytometry Assay:** A more sensitive and quantitative test for CGD. DHR is oxidized to fluorescent rhodamine by ROS produced during the respiratory burst. CGD neutrophils show minimal or no fluorescence.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Defective synthesis of superoxide dismutase (SOD): SOD converts superoxide (O2-) to hydrogen peroxide (H2O2). A defect in SOD would lead to an accumulation of superoxide and potentially oxidative damage, but it would not impair the initial production of ROS by NADPH oxidase. Patients with SOD deficiency typically do not present with the classic CGD phenotype of recurrent catalase-positive infections.
- (B) Defective synthesis of catalase: Catalase breaks down hydrogen peroxide (H2O2). A defect in catalase would lead to an accumulation of H2O2, which could potentially cause oxidative damage but would not impair the phagocyte's ability to kill pathogens via the respiratory burst. In fact, catalase deficiency is rare and generally not associated with increased susceptibility to infection; it might even be protective against certain ROS-mediated damage.
- (D) Defective synthesis of the complement component C5: C5 deficiency impairs the formation of the membrane attack complex (MAC) and C5a production. This leads to increased susceptibility primarily to *Neisseria* species (meningitis, gonorrhea) due to impaired killing via MAC, and impaired neutrophil chemotaxis due to lack of C5a. It does not cause a defect in the phagocyte respiratory burst or susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms like *S. aureus* and *Aspergillus*.
- (E) Defective synthesis of the Fc receptor gamma chain (Fcγc): The Fcγc (encoded by *FCGR2B*) is part of the FcγRII receptor, which binds IgG. Defects in Fcγc lead to impaired antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC) and phagocytosis. This results in susceptibility to encapsulated bacteria and viruses, but not the specific pattern of catalase-positive infections and defective respiratory burst seen in CGD.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | SOD Deficiency | Catalase Deficiency | C5 Deficiency | Fcγc Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :------------- | :------------------ | :------------ | :-------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH Oxidase Complex                | SOD Enzyme     | Catalase Enzyme     | C5 Protein    | Fcγc Protein    |
| **Key Mechanism**       | Impaired Respiratory Burst (ROS)     | ROS Accumulation | H2O2 Accumulation  | No MAC/C5a    | Impaired ADCC/Phagocytosis |
| **NBT Test**            | Negative (No Blue)                   | Positive       | Positive            | Positive      | Positive        |
| **Typical Infections**  | Catalase+ Bacteria/Fungi (*S. aureus*, *Aspergillus*) | Rare/Unclear   | Rare/Unclear       | *Neisseria*   | Encapsulated Bacteria/Viruses |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR           | AR             | AR                  | AR            | AR              |
| **Granuloma Formation** | Yes                                  | No             | No                  | No            | No              |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defective phagocyte oxidative burst due to mutations in NADPH oxidase subunits, leading to recurrent infections with catalase-positive organisms. The NBT test is a classic diagnostic tool. Differentiate CGD from other phagocyte defects (e.g., Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency - LAD, Chediak-Higashi syndrome) and other immunodeficiencies based on specific pathogens, laboratory findings, and mechanisms. Remember the key pathogens (*S. aureus*, *Aspergillus*, *Serratia*, *Burkholderia*, *Nocardia*) and the role of catalase.

<a id='question-096'></a>

### Question 096: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) is a primary immunodeficiency characterized by defects in the NADPH oxidase complex, leading to impaired phagocyte oxidative burst and susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. (慢性肉芽肿病是一种原发性免疫缺陷病，其特征是 NADPH 氧化酶复合物的缺陷，导致吞噬细胞的氧化爆发受损，并易感于 catalase 阳性微生物。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections, including pneumonia, skin abscesses, and lymphadenitis. His mother reports that he has had multiple hospitalizations for similar infections since infancy, often requiring intravenous antibiotics and surgical drainage. Family history is significant for a maternal uncle who died at age 10 from overwhelming sepsis. Physical examination reveals multiple draining skin lesions, hepatosplenomegaly, and cervical lymphadenopathy. Laboratory studies show leukocytosis with neutrophilia. A skin biopsy from an abscess reveals numerous neutrophils surrounding necrotic tissue, but these neutrophils lack the ability to produce reactive oxygen species (ROS). Flow cytometry analysis of neutrophils shows normal expression of CD11b/CD18 (integrin) and CD16 (FcγRIII), ruling out leukocyte adhesion deficiency (LAD) and FcγRIII deficiency, respectively. A dihydrorhodamine (DHR) flow cytometry assay demonstrates absent oxidative burst in neutrophils upon stimulation with phorbol myristate acetate (PMA). Which of the following is the most likely underlying defect in this patient?

(A) Defective assembly of the C5b-9 membrane attack complex (MAC)
(B) Impaired intracellular killing due to deficient lysosomal trafficking
(C) Defective phagocyte migration due to impaired integrin function
(D) Deficient production of superoxide anions due to a mutation in a component of the NADPH oxidase complex
(E) Impaired opsonization due to deficiency in complement component C3

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 6-year-old boy with recurrent, severe bacterial and fungal infections (pneumonia, skin abscesses, lymphadenitis) since infancy. This pattern suggests a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocytic function or cell-mediated immunity.
- **Family History:** Maternal uncle died young from sepsis, suggesting a possible X-linked inheritance pattern.
- **Physical Exam:** Multiple draining skin lesions, hepatosplenomegaly, lymphadenopathy – consistent with chronic inflammation and infection.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukocytosis with neutrophilia indicates an active inflammatory response, but the neutrophils are functionally impaired.
- **Skin Biopsy:** Neutrophils present but unable to produce ROS, pointing towards a defect in the oxidative burst mechanism.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Normal CD11b/CD18 (integrin) rules out LAD. Normal CD16 (FcγRIII) rules out FcγRIII deficiency.
- **DHR Assay:** Absent oxidative burst upon PMA stimulation confirms a defect in ROS production by neutrophils.
- **Overall Picture:** The constellation of recurrent catalase-positive bacterial and fungal infections, family history suggestive of X-linked inheritance, and confirmed absence of neutrophil oxidative burst strongly points to Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD).

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD):** CGD is a primary immunodeficiency affecting phagocytes (neutrophils, monocytes, macrophages).
- **Pathophysiology:** The core defect lies in the NADPH oxidase complex, a multi-subunit enzyme complex located in the phagosomal membrane. This complex is responsible for generating superoxide anions (O2•−) from molecular oxygen (O2) during the respiratory burst. Superoxide is then converted to other ROS, such as hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) and hypochlorous acid (HOCl), which are crucial for killing ingested pathogens, especially catalase-positive organisms.
- **NADPH Oxidase Complex:** Consists of several subunits, including gp91phox (encoded by the *CYBB* gene on the X chromosome, accounting for most cases), p22phox (encoded by *CYBA*), p47phox (encoded by *NCF1*), p67phox (encoded by *NCF2*), and p40phox (encoded by *NCF4*). Mutations in any of these genes can cause CGD. The most common form is X-linked (gp91phox deficiency).
- **Consequences of Defect:** Without functional NADPH oxidase, phagocytes cannot produce ROS. This impairs their ability to kill certain bacteria (e.g., *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Serratia marcescens*, *Burkholderia cepacia*, *Nocardia*) and fungi (e.g., *Aspergillus*, *Candida*), particularly those that produce catalase, which breaks down H2O2.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** Recurrent, severe infections (pneumonia, abscesses, osteomyelitis, lymphadenitis) often caused by catalase-positive organisms. Granuloma formation is common due to the body's attempt to wall off persistent infections. Autoimmune manifestations can also occur.
- **Diagnosis:** Dihydrorhodamine (DHR) test or nitroblue tetrazolium (NBT) test measures the oxidative burst activity of neutrophils. Flow cytometry is used to assess DHR oxidation. Genetic testing identifies specific mutations.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective assembly of the C5b-9 membrane attack complex (MAC):** This describes deficiencies in the terminal complement pathway (e.g., C5-C9 deficiencies). Patients typically present with recurrent infections by *Neisseria* species (meningitis, gonorrhea). This does not fit the clinical picture of recurrent bacterial/fungal abscesses and the specific finding of impaired neutrophil oxidative burst.
- (B) **Impaired intracellular killing due to deficient lysosomal trafficking:** This describes Chédiak-Higashi syndrome, characterized by defective lysosomal fusion and trafficking due to mutations in the *LYST* gene. Patients have partial oculocutaneous albinism, recurrent pyogenic infections, giant granules in leukocytes, and progressive neurological deterioration. The defect is not in the oxidative burst itself, and the clinical features (albinism, giant granules) are absent in the vignette.
- (C) **Defective phagocyte migration due to impaired integrin function:** This describes Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD). LAD type 1 is caused by mutations in the *ITGB2* gene encoding the CD18 subunit of β2 integrins (like CD11b/CD18). Patients have recurrent bacterial infections, impaired wound healing, and delayed umbilical cord separation. The vignette explicitly states that CD11b/CD18 expression is normal on flow cytometry, ruling out LAD.
- (E) **Impaired opsonization due to deficiency in complement component C3:** C3 deficiency is a severe immunodeficiency affecting both classical and alternative complement pathways. Patients have recurrent infections with encapsulated bacteria (e.g., *Streptococcus pneumoniae*, *Haemophilus influenzae*) early in life. While phagocytosis is impaired, the primary defect is not in the phagocyte's intrinsic killing mechanism (oxidative burst), and the specific DHR test result would be normal.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD) | Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD) | Complement C3 Deficiency | Chédiak-Higashi Syndrome | Terminal Complement Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------- | :--------------------------------- | :----------------------- | :------------------------ | :----------------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | NADPH oxidase complex               | β2 integrin (CD18)                 | C3 protein               | LYST gene (lysosomal trafficking) | C5-C9 complex                  |
| **Cell Affected**       | Phagocytes (Neutrophils)            | Phagocytes (Neutrophils)           | All cells                | Phagocytes, Melanocytes   | All cells                      |
| **Key Mechanism Defect** | Impaired oxidative burst (ROS)      | Impaired adhesion/migration        | Impaired opsonization/MAC | Impaired lysosomal fusion | Impaired MAC formation         |
| **Typical Infections**  | Catalase+ bacteria, Fungi          | Encapsulated bacteria              | Encapsulated bacteria    | Pyogenic bacteria         | *Neisseria* species             |
| **Key Lab Finding**     | Absent DHR/NBT test                 | Absent/Reduced CD11b/CD18          | Low C3 levels            | Giant granules in WBCs    | Low C5-C9 levels                |
| **Inheritance**         | X-linked (most common), AR          | AR                                 | AR                       | AR                        | AR/X-linked                    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CGD is a phagocyte defect characterized by an inability to generate ROS due to mutations in NADPH oxidase subunits. This leads to susceptibility to catalase-positive organisms. Key diagnostic tests include the DHR assay and flow cytometry. Differentiate CGD from other phagocyte defects like LAD (impaired migration/adhesion, normal oxidative burst) and Chédiak-Higashi (lysosomal trafficking defect, giant granules). Remember the specific organisms associated with each defect.

<a id='question-097'></a>

### Question 097: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 47 (chemotactic)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular basis of leukocyte adhesion deficiency type 1 (LAD-1), specifically the defect in the β2 integrin subunit (CD18), leading to impaired leukocyte adhesion, chemotaxis, and phagocytosis, resulting in recurrent bacterial infections and impaired wound healing. (本题考察白细胞黏附缺陷1型 (LAD-1) 的分子基础，即 β2 整合素亚基 (CD18) 的缺陷，导致白细胞黏附、趋化和吞噬功能受损，从而引起复发性细菌感染和伤口愈合不良。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-month-old male infant presents to the emergency department with a 2-week history of persistent omphalitis (infection of the umbilical stump) that has failed to respond to multiple courses of broad-spectrum antibiotics. He also has delayed separation of the umbilical cord (separation occurred at 3 weeks, normal is < 2 weeks). His mother reports recurrent skin infections, including cellulitis and abscesses, and poor wound healing after minor trauma. Physical examination reveals a pale, afebrile infant with a non-healing, erythematous, purulent umbilical stump. Laboratory investigations show marked leukocytosis (neutrophil count 25,000/µL) but normal neutrophil morphology and function *in vitro* (e.g., oxidative burst). Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood leukocytes reveals absent expression of CD18 (β2 integrin subunit) on neutrophils, monocytes, and lymphocytes.

(A) Defective NADPH oxidase activity leading to impaired respiratory burst.
(B) Impaired chemotaxis due to a defect in the C5a receptor (CD87).
(C) Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) affecting multiple cytokine receptors.
(D) Defective expression of the β2 integrin subunit (CD18) impairing leukocyte adhesion.
(E) Defective production of mannose-binding lectin (MBL) leading to impaired complement activation.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Age and Presentation:** A 6-month-old infant presenting with recurrent, severe bacterial infections (omphalitis, cellulitis, abscesses) and delayed umbilical cord separation points towards a primary immunodeficiency affecting innate immunity, particularly neutrophil function.
- **Delayed Umbilical Cord Separation:** This is a classic hallmark sign of Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 1 (LAD-1). Normally, neutrophils migrate to the umbilical stump, causing inflammation and separation. Impaired neutrophil migration leads to delayed separation.
- **Recurrent Bacterial Infections:** The types of infections (skin, umbilical stump) are typical for defects in neutrophil function, as neutrophils are crucial for combating extracellular bacteria.
- **Poor Wound Healing:** Neutrophils are essential for the initial inflammatory phase of wound healing, clearing debris and bacteria. Their dysfunction impairs this process.
- **Leukocytosis:** High neutrophil count suggests the bone marrow is producing neutrophils, but they cannot effectively reach the sites of infection due to a functional defect.
- **Normal *in vitro* Neutrophil Function (Oxidative Burst):** This rules out defects in intracellular killing mechanisms like Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD), where the oxidative burst is impaired.
- **Flow Cytometry: Absent CD18:** This is the definitive diagnostic finding for LAD-1. CD18 is the β2 subunit common to several integrins (LFA-1, Mac-1, p150,95) crucial for leukocyte adhesion to endothelial cells and extravasation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency Type 1 (LAD-1):** This is an autosomal recessive disorder caused by mutations in the *ITGB2* gene, which encodes the CD18 protein (β2 integrin subunit).
- **CD18 Function:** CD18 forms heterodimers with different α subunits (e.g., CD11a, CD11b, CD11c) to create integrins like LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18), Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18), and p150,95 (CD11c/CD18). These integrins are expressed on leukocytes.
- **Mechanism of Defect:** Integrins mediate the firm adhesion of leukocytes to endothelial cells, a critical step in extravasation (migration from blood vessels into tissues). They also play roles in phagocytosis and cell-cell interactions. The absence of CD18 prevents the formation of functional β2 integrins.
- **Consequences:** Without functional β2 integrins, neutrophils cannot firmly adhere to endothelium, migrate out of blood vessels towards chemotactic signals (chemotaxis is impaired *in vivo* due to inability to reach the site), and effectively phagocytose particles.
- **Clinical Manifestations:** Recurrent bacterial infections (especially skin, mucosal surfaces, umbilical stump), delayed umbilical cord separation (>3 weeks), impaired wound healing, and often severe periodontitis.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Marked neutrophilia (leukocytosis), normal neutrophil morphology and *in vitro* function (e.g., oxidative burst, chemotaxis in a static assay), absent CD18 expression on leukocytes by flow cytometry.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Defective NADPH oxidase activity leading to impaired respiratory burst:** This describes Chronic Granulomatous Disease (CGD). Patients with CGD have recurrent infections, particularly with catalase-positive organisms (e.g., *Staphylococcus aureus*, *Aspergillus*), and form granulomas. The dihydrorhodamine (DHR) test or nitroblue tetrazolium (NBT) test would be abnormal, and flow cytometry would show absent or reduced CD67 expression (a marker of NADPH oxidase activation). The vignette specifically states normal *in vitro* neutrophil function, ruling this out.
- (B) **Impaired chemotaxis due to a defect in the C5a receptor (CD87):** Defects in chemotaxis can occur, but a primary defect in the C5a receptor (as seen in some forms of Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 2 or specific receptor deficiencies) would typically present differently. LAD-1 primarily affects adhesion and extravasation, which *secondarily* impairs *in vivo* chemotaxis. Furthermore, the key finding here is the absent CD18, not a C5a receptor defect. While chemotaxis is impaired in LAD-1, the *primary* defect is adhesion.
- (C) **Defective synthesis of the common gamma chain (γc) affecting multiple cytokine receptors:** This describes X-linked Severe Combined Immunodeficiency (X-SCID). Patients with X-SCID have profound defects in T cell and NK cell development due to the lack of γc, which is essential for signaling through receptors for IL-2, IL-4, IL-7, IL-9, IL-15, and IL-21. They present early in infancy with severe opportunistic infections (viral, fungal, bacterial) and failure to thrive. The vignette describes a specific defect in neutrophil function, not a broad T/NK cell deficiency.
- (E) **Defective production of mannose-binding lectin (MBL) leading to impaired complement activation:** MBL deficiency is a relatively common immunodeficiency that can increase susceptibility to infections, particularly encapsulated bacteria. However, it does not cause delayed umbilical cord separation or the specific pattern of severe bacterial skin/mucosal infections seen in LAD-1. The hallmark features of LAD-1 (delayed cord separation, severe neutrophilia, absent CD18) are not associated with MBL deficiency.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | LAD-1 (CD18 Defect) | CGD (NADPH Oxidase Defect) | X-SCID (γc Defect) | MBL Deficiency |
| :---------------------- | :------------------ | :------------------------- | :---------------- | :------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | CD18 (β2 integrin)  | NADPH Oxidase              | Common γ chain    | MBL protein    |
| **Cell Type Affected**  | Neutrophils, Monocytes, Lymphocytes | Neutrophils, Monocytes | T cells, NK cells | Macrophages, etc. |
| **Key Clinical Sign**   | Delayed cord separation | Granuloma formation        | Severe early infections | Increased encapsulated bacterial infections |
| **Lab Finding**         | Absent CD18         | Abnormal DHR/NBT test      | Low T/NK cells    | Low MBL levels |
| **Neutrophil Function** | Impaired adhesion/extravasation | Impaired oxidative burst | Normal            | Normal         |
| **Leukocyte Count**     | High (Neutrophilia) | Normal/High                | Low               | Normal         |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 1 (LAD-1) is caused by a defect in the CD18 subunit of β2 integrins, leading to impaired leukocyte adhesion, migration, and phagocytosis. Key clinical features include delayed umbilical cord separation, recurrent bacterial infections without pus formation, and impaired wound healing. The diagnosis is confirmed by flow cytometry showing absent CD18 expression. Differentiate LAD-1 from CGD (impaired oxidative burst, granulomas), X-SCID (severe T/NK cell deficiency, early opportunistic infections), and MBL deficiency (increased risk of encapsulated bacterial infections).

### Question 098: Primary Immunodeficiencies: Part 2 Focus 48 (degranulation)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Defective degranulation in cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs) and natural killer (NK) cells due to mutations in genes encoding proteins involved in the regulated release of cytotoxic granules (perforin, granzymes) leads to impaired killing of infected cells and tumor cells, resulting in susceptibility to specific infections and malignancies. (细胞毒性T淋巴细胞和自然杀伤细胞的脱颗粒缺陷，由参与细胞毒性颗粒（穿孔素、颗粒酶）调节释放的基因突变引起，导致杀伤受损细胞和肿瘤细胞的能力下降，从而易感于特定感染和恶性肿瘤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents with recurrent, severe viral infections, including varicella pneumonia and disseminated herpes simplex virus (HSV) infection, starting in infancy. He also has a history of multiple episodes of pneumonia caused by *Pneumocystis jirovecii*. Family history is significant for a similar presentation in his older brother, who died at age 5 from a rapidly progressive lymphoma. Physical examination reveals failure to thrive and chronic diarrhea. Immunological workup shows normal lymphocyte counts but markedly reduced cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL) and natural killer (NK) cell activity in response to viral targets. Flow cytometry reveals normal expression of CD3, CD8, CD56, and granzyme B on the cell surface, but intracellular staining shows significantly reduced levels of granzyme B within the cells. Furthermore, electron microscopy of NK cells shows abnormally large, dense granules that fail to fuse with the plasma membrane upon target cell engagement. Genetic testing confirms a homozygous mutation in the *PRF1* gene.

Which of the following cellular processes is most directly impaired in this patient?
(A) Phagosome-lysosome fusion in macrophages
(B) Formation of the immunological synapse between T cells and antigen-presenting cells
(C) Exocytosis of cytotoxic granules by CTLs and NK cells
(D) Antigen presentation via MHC class I molecules on infected cells
(E) Complement-mediated lysis of target cells

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent severe viral infections (varicella, HSV):** Suggests a defect in cell-mediated immunity, specifically involving CTLs and NK cells, which are crucial for controlling viral infections.
- ***Pneumocystis jirovecii* pneumonia:** While often associated with T helper cell defects (like DiGeorge syndrome or HIV), severe CTL/NK cell defects can also predispose to opportunistic infections like PCP.
- **Family history of similar illness and lymphoma:** Points towards a genetic, inherited immunodeficiency. Lymphoma susceptibility is a known feature of certain primary immunodeficiencies involving impaired immune surveillance.
- **Normal lymphocyte counts:** Rules out severe lymphopenia syndromes like SCID.
- **Reduced CTL and NK cell activity:** Directly implicates these effector cells in the immunodeficiency.
- **Normal surface CD markers (CD3, CD8, CD56) and granzyme B expression:** Indicates that the cells are present and express the necessary surface markers and the cytotoxic effector molecule, but something is wrong with its function.
- **Reduced intracellular granzyme B:** Suggests a problem with the intracellular processing or trafficking of granzyme B, or its release.
- **Abnormally large, dense granules failing to fuse with the plasma membrane:** This is the key finding. It points directly to a defect in the exocytosis pathway required for releasing the granule contents (perforin and granzymes) into the target cell.
- ***PRF1* gene mutation:** *PRF1* encodes perforin. Perforin is essential for forming pores in the target cell membrane, allowing granzymes to enter and induce apoptosis. While the vignette mentions granzyme B issues, the *PRF1* mutation directly affects perforin, which is crucial for the *delivery* mechanism of granzymes. However, the overall process described (granules failing to fuse and release contents) is the core defect, which is impaired exocytosis. The vignette describes a defect in granule *fusion* and release, which is the exocytosis step.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **CTL and NK cell killing:** These cells recognize and kill infected or cancerous cells. They form an immunological synapse with the target cell and release cytotoxic granules containing perforin and granzymes.
- **Perforin:** A pore-forming protein that polymerizes on the target cell membrane, creating channels.
- **Granzymes:** Serine proteases (like granzyme B) that enter the target cell through perforin pores and activate caspases, triggering apoptosis.
- **Exocytosis:** The process by which intracellular vesicles (like cytotoxic granules) fuse with the plasma membrane and release their contents outside the cell. This process is tightly regulated and involves SNARE proteins (e.g., syntaxin, SNAP-25, VAMP/synaptobrevin) and calcium signaling.
- **PRF1 gene:** Encodes perforin. Mutations lead to Familial Hemophagocytic Lymphohistiocytosis type 2 (HLH2) or Chediak-Higashi syndrome-like phenotype (though Chediak-Higashi is typically associated with *LYST* mutations affecting lysosomal trafficking). Defective perforin function impairs CTL and NK cell killing, leading to uncontrolled T cell activation, cytokine storm, hemophagocytosis, and susceptibility to infections and lymphoma.
- **The vignette describes a defect in granule fusion and release:** This is the definition of impaired exocytosis of cytotoxic granules. While the *PRF1* mutation affects perforin, the *functional* consequence described (granules not fusing/releasing) is the impaired exocytosis step itself. The question asks about the *process* most directly impaired.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Phagosome-lysosome fusion in macrophages:** This process is defective in Chediak-Higashi syndrome (due to *LYST* mutations), leading to impaired bacterial killing by phagocytes. While this patient has recurrent infections, the primary defect described involves CTLs and NK cells, and the specific granule defect points away from phagocyte function.
- (B) **Formation of the immunological synapse between T cells and antigen-presenting cells:** This is crucial for T cell activation. Defects here would impair T cell priming and proliferation, not primarily the effector function (killing) of already activated CTLs. The vignette describes reduced *activity* of CTLs/NK cells, not a failure of synapse formation.
- (D) **Antigen presentation via MHC class I molecules on infected cells:** This is essential for CTL recognition. Defects here (e.g., TAP1/2 deficiency, mutations in MHC class I genes) would prevent CTLs from recognizing infected cells, leading to susceptibility to intracellular pathogens. However, the vignette states that CTLs have reduced activity *in response to viral targets*, implying recognition is occurring, but killing is impaired. Also, the specific granule defect points away from antigen presentation.
- (E) **Complement-mediated lysis of target cells:** This is a mechanism of innate immunity involving the complement cascade forming a membrane attack complex (MAC) on pathogens. It is distinct from CTL/NK cell-mediated cytotoxicity, which relies on perforin/granzymes. The clinical picture (severe viral infections, lymphoma) and the specific cellular defect (CTL/NK granule exocytosis) do not point to a complement deficiency.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                     | Impaired CTL/NK Exocytosis (PRF1 mutation, vignette) | Chediak-Higashi (LYST mutation) | TAP1/2 Deficiency | MHC Class I Deficiency | Complement Deficiency |
| :-------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------ | :--------------- | :--------------------- | :-------------------- |
| **Primary Cell Defect**     | CTLs, NK cells                                       | Phagocytes, CTLs, NK cells      | All nucleated cells | All nucleated cells    | Liver (mostly)        |
| **Defective Process**       | Granule exocytosis (fusion/release)                   | Lysosomal trafficking, phagocytosis, granule exocytosis | Peptide transport into ER | MHC I expression      | Complement cascade   |
| **Key Molecules Affected**  | Perforin (PRF1), SNAREs, granule trafficking        | LYST protein                    | TAP1, TAP2       | MHC Class I genes     | Complement proteins  |
| **Clinical Manifestations** | Severe viral infections, lymphoma, HLH2               | Recurrent pyogenic infections, partial oculocutaneous albinism, neurological issues, impaired granule exocytosis | Susceptibility to intracellular bacteria/viruses (e.g., Mycobacteria, CMV) | Susceptibility to intracellular viruses (e.g., CMV, HSV) | Recurrent pyogenic infections (Neisseria) |
| **Granule Morphology**      | Abnormally large, dense, fail to fuse                | Abnormally large, lysosomes     | Normal          | Normal                | Normal                |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Defects in CTL and NK cell cytotoxic granule exocytosis, often due to mutations affecting perforin (*PRF1*) or SNARE proteins, impair the release of perforin and granzymes, leading to defective killing of infected or malignant cells. This results in susceptibility to severe viral infections and lymphoma. Differentiate this from Chediak-Higashi syndrome (LYST mutation, affects phagosome-lysosome fusion and granule exocytosis in multiple cell types, associated with large granules and albinism) and defects in antigen presentation (TAP deficiency, MHC deficiency) or other immune mechanisms (complement deficiency).
